\begin{Indent}\textbf{ Methods for R3 Blocks}\par
{\em This section contain the methods that allow to implement one of the most innovative features of S\+M\+S++\+: that of \char`\"{}\+R3 Blocks\char`\"{}. The idea is that each (derived class from) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may (but need not necessarily) be capable of producing \char`\"{}\+Block of different types that encode problems meaningfully
related to the original one\char`\"{}. These are typically \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that encode\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item A Reformulation, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that encodes a problem whose optimal solutions are optimal also for the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, while being for some reason \char`\"{}more convenient to solve\char`\"{} by some specific algorithm. Note that Reformulations may be defined over a completely different space of Variables, provided that some appropriate mapping can be defined between the original and reformulated space. The mapping need not be algebraic, but must obviously be algorithmic \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}} below\mbox{]}.
\item A Relaxation, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} whose optimal value provides a valid global lower bound (for a minimization problem, upper bound for a maximization one) on the optimal value of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This is typically obtained by having a larger feasible region (e.\+g., relaxing some constraints) and/or an objective function whose value is smaller than the original one (for a minimization problem, larger for a maximization one) on the original feasible region. One also expects that the Relaxation is easier to solve than the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Usually, a(n algorithmic) map between solutions of the Relaxation and those of the original problem is also available, although solutions of the Relaxation may clearly not be feasible for the original problem (but they may as well be; in this case, if the objective value also coincides, such a solution is actually optimal for the original problem).
\item A Restriction, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} whose optimal value provides a valid global upper bound (for a minimization problem, lower bound for a maximization one) on the optimal value of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This is typically obtained by having a smaller feasible region (e.\+g., adding some constraints, as in fixing some variables) and/or an objective function whose value is larger than the original one (for a minimization problem, smaller for a maximization one) on the original feasible region. One also expects that the Restriction is easier to solve than the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Again, a(n algorithmic) map between solutions of the Restriction and those of the original problem is also usually available, and these solutions are most often feasible for the original problem.
\end{DoxyItemize}

These three kinds of \char`\"{}derived\char`\"{} Blocks are often crucial to derive efficient (as much as possible) solution methods for the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. The issue is that there are very many ways to produce R3 Blocks, some of which requiring rather complex maps between the variables space of the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and the original one. In order to allow arbitrarily maps to be used, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class does not make any assumption on how the map is done, except that\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item The original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can map the current solution from an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to a solution of itself \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} below\mbox{]} and vice-\/versa \mbox{[}see and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}} below\mbox{]}. There is no guarantee that the mapped back solution be feasible, say if the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is a relaxation, nor that the mapped forward solution is, say if the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is a restriction.
\item The original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can in principle \char`\"{}translate\char`\"{} a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} occurring to itself to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} of the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a26eeed482e287e1f8a384826c789f9e0}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification()}}. A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can freely decide which \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} it will map.
\item The original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can in principle \char`\"{}translate\char`\"{} a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} occurring to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} of itself, see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9524f314b064aafbd8d2f8a398b797a2}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+\+Modification()}}. A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can freely decide which \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} it will map.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that, in order to be able to perform these tasks, the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to be able to access possibly everything in the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Hence, the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will either have to make the (specific derived class of) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} friend, or will have to provide methods for accessing all the necessary data structures, or to make them public outright. }\begin{DoxyCompactItemize}
\item 
virtual \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$ \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block}} (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$r3bc=nullptr)
\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em produces a new R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \end{DoxyCompactList}\item 
virtual void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution}} (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$R3B, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$r3bc=nullptr, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$solc=nullptr)
\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em maps back solution information from an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \end{DoxyCompactList}\item 
virtual void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution}} (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$R3B, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$r3bc=nullptr, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$solc=nullptr)
\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em maps forward solution information from the original Blocka to n R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \end{DoxyCompactList}\item 
virtual void \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a26eeed482e287e1f8a384826c789f9e0}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification}} (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$R3B, \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga9024cf2ead61a1280d29771b063b1a62}{sp\+\_\+\+Mod}} mod, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$r3bc=nullptr, \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}} issue\+P\+Mod=\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4ada69e89c5f46e563d8a13cfa6830c136}{e\+No\+Blck}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}} issue\+A\+Mod=\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4a21b14b26b1c0093a0905f6fc010bad29}{e\+Mod\+Blck}})
\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em maps forward a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} from the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \end{DoxyCompactList}\item

\subsection*{Protected Member Functions}
\begin{Indent}\textbf{ Protected methods for handling the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{}}\par
{\em The following methods are the only ones that derived classes can use to manipulate the four vectors v\+\_\+s\+\_\+\+Constraint, v\+\_\+s\+\_\+\+Variable, v\+\_\+d\+\_\+\+Constraint, v\+\_\+s\+\_\+\+Variable, that are purposely {\itshape private}. This ensures that only pointers of the right type can be found there; these being vectors of boost\+::any, it would be very easy for derived classes to accidentally put there pointers of the wrong type. Also, the methods ensure that the v\+\_\+\+X\+\_\+\+Y\+\_\+names vectors are kept of the same size as the corresponding v\+\_\+\+X\+\_\+Y ones (giving the newly added stuff no name\+: the v\+\_\+\+X\+\_\+\+Y\+\_\+names vectors are protected, so derived classes can mess up with them later at their leisure). }\begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9e42f7f19c1e96b86ab3978d86ab9c0f}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9e42f7f19c1e96b86ab3978d86ab9c0f}}

\begin{Indent}\textbf{ Protected methods for handling Solver list}\par
{\em These are the protected versions of the same-\/named public methods, which take non-\/const iterators\+: they do the brunt of the job avoiding to cast away the const-\/ness. \begin{Indent}\textbf{ Protected methods for inserting and extracting}\par
{\em The \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides two pairs of symmetric \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e7b9c9148a98c5c342e8c25cac95926}{print()}} / \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a27ae9e4279482d52342c7958c34a43c6}{load()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a8209df4072912666c1ea5f6979c035fc}{serialize()}} / \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4210de3b3b076cf4273eaee2b916da84}{deserialize()}} methods to save information about it on a std\+::stream / net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group and retrieve it. For \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e7b9c9148a98c5c342e8c25cac95926}{print()}} the saved information may or may not be enough to fully reconstruct the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} via \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a27ae9e4279482d52342c7958c34a43c6}{load()}}, depending on the verbosity level, while for \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a8209df4072912666c1ea5f6979c035fc}{serialize()}} the saved information is always enough to fully reconstruct the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} via \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4210de3b3b076cf4273eaee2b916da84}{deserialize()}}.

For conditions that need be respected by \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a27ae9e4279482d52342c7958c34a43c6}{load()}} see the comments to deserialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group \&\& ), while for those that need be respected by \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e7b9c9148a98c5c342e8c25cac95926}{print()}} see comments to serialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group \&\& ). }\begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

The base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class also defines the interface for serializing and de-\/serializing a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} onto net\+C\+DF files. \subsection{Detailed Description}
the cornerstone of the S\+M\+S++ system\+: a class for \char`\"{}a block in a model\char`\"{} 

The \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class is the cornerstone of the S\+M\+S++ system. It is meant to represent the basic concept of a \char`\"{}block\char`\"{} in a block-\/structured mathematical model. In general a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} contains\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item any number of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_variable_8h}{Variable.\+h}}\mbox{]};
\item any number of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_constraint_8h}{Constraint.\+h}}\mbox{]};
\item one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_objective_8h}{Objective.\+h}}\mbox{]};
\item any number of (inner) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Furthermore, it is possibly contained into a father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and Constraints can either be static (i.\+e., they are guaranteed to be always there throughout the life of the model, although of course the value that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} attain is, well, variable) or dynamic (i.\+e., that may appear and disappear during the life of the model). Conversely, the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of a given \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are assumed to be always static, i.\+e., their number and type cannot change. One of the main design decisions in S\+M\+S++ is that the \char`\"{}name\char`\"{} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} or \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} is just its memory address; this means that, once constructed, they cannot be moved to a different memory location. Hence, the difference between static and dynamic stuff is that only the former can live into arrays, provided that the arrays are {\itshape never shortened or lengthened} (for doing so may cause the memory to be re-\/allocated, which would change the address violating the basic assumption). Conversely, dynamic stuff can only live into lists, so that elements can be added or removed without causing other elements to be reallocated.

Yet, both static and dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} in a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be arranged in any number of \char`\"{}groups\char`\"{}, each of which can be a multi-\/dimensional array with (in principle) an arbitrary number of dimensions. For dynamic Variable/\+Constraint, only the last dimension can by varying. \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} relies onto boost\+::any to be able to handle vector of variables with an arbitrary number of indices, and to boost\+::multi\+\_\+array to implement them. Tools are provided so that both (multi-\/dimensional vectors of lists of) {\itshape any possible derived class} from Variable/\+Constraint, as well as (multi-\/dimensional vectors of lists of) {\itshape pointers} to {\itshape any possible derived class} from Variable/\+Constraint, can be handled.

\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is a {\itshape base} class for representing the general concept of \char`\"{}a part of
a mathematical model with a well-\/understood identity\char`\"{}; in other words, it is expected that it will be used to define {\itshape derived} classes, each of which represents a model with a specific structure (say, a Knapsack, a Traveling Salesman Problem, a Semi\+Definite program, ...). be represented as a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Hence, the Variables and Constraints of such a specific model will be organized into appropriate, \char`\"{}natural\char`\"{} (multi-\/dimensional) vectors. Actually, the Constraint/\+Objective can then be represented {\itshape implicitly} by just providing the data that characterizes them (the weights and costs of the item in a knapsack, an annotated graph, the size of a square semidefinite matrix, ...). Such data (characterizing the instance of the problem) will be available as fields/methods of the derived class. We call this the \char`\"{}physical representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

The reason for wanting to define a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is to have the corresponding mathematical model solved. For this reason, the general interface class \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_solver_8h}{Solver.\+h}}\mbox{]} is defined. The idea is that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be attached to any number of {\itshape appropriate} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}, i.\+e., that are able to solve the corresponding mathematical model. All solvers are specialized, i.\+e., they are only able to solve a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with specific characteristics (this is obviously so because, basically, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is completely generic and can represent anything, and there is no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} that can solve anything). So, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} will only accept to be attached to specific sub-\/classes of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Hence, the specific data for that specific sub-\/class will be visible to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}, which could in principle only need it, and {\itshape not} the Constraint/\+Objective of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, to solve the corresponding problem. Thus, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class supports the notion that the constraints and objective may not necessarily be explicitly defined (in terms of the corresponding Constraint/\+Objective objects. Conversely, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} are in principle always needed, as they are a crucial part of the interface between the user of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}\+: the latter write their solutions into the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, so that the user of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can read them. However, any specialized \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can also have a specialied way to represent its solution, and a user of that particular \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can conceptually only use that. Indees, the status at any point in time of the solution of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be saved into a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_solution_8h}{Solution.\+h}}\mbox{]}, and read it back from a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object; this need not necessarily be expressed in terms of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (although of course it has to be written in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} if they are defined). Since \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} can have dual information attached to them, this can similarly be saved.

However, Blocks can also be attached to general-\/purpose solvers that only need the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and Constraint/\+Objective to be of some specific type (say, single real numbers and affine functions, ...). Hence, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides a mechanism whereby, upon request, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \char`\"{}exhibits\char`\"{} its Constraint/\+Objective and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} as an \char`\"{}unstructured\char`\"{} list of \mbox{[}multi-\/dimensional arrays of\mbox{]} \mbox{[}lists of\mbox{]} \mbox{[}pointers to\mbox{]} objects of class (derived from) Constraint/\+Objective and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}; we call this the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This representation somewhat abstracts away from the very specific structure of the problem, allowing to see it as just an \char`\"{}unstructured\char`\"{} instance of some very general class.

\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and Constraint/\+Objective in a Blocks are in principle dynamic (even static ones), in the sense that, at the very least, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} can take different values and can be fixed/unfixed, while Constraints can be relaxed/enforced. Because a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be attached to several \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} at the same time, changes in its components are handled in a \char`\"{}lazy\char`\"{} way. That is, each time a change occurs, an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_modification_8h}{Modification.\+h}}\mbox{]} is dispatched to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which is why \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} derives from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}}. Other classes, like \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_f_row_constraint}{F\+Row\+Constraint}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_f_real_objective}{F\+Real\+Objective}} are \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} in order to allow \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_function}{Function}} to pass them \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} that are ultimately dispatched to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} in turn dispatches them to all \char`\"{}interested\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}, so that the next time they are required to solve the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} they know which changes have occurred since the last time they have solved it, and therefore are able to react accordingly (hopefully re-\/optimizing to improve the efficiency of the solution process). A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} is \char`\"{}interested\char`\"{} in a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} only if the latter occurs aither in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to which it is attached or in one of its sub-\/\+Block (recursively), and only if is has occurred after it was attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and since the last time it solved the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} has the responsibility of always cleaning up its list of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}, because it is passed {\itshape smart poimters} and therefore a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is only deleted when the last \char`\"{}interested\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} it has been passed to deletes its smart pointer. A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} can refer to either the \char`\"{}physical representation\char`\"{} or the \char`\"{}abstract
representation\char`\"{} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which means that actually a single change may cause more than one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to be issued to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}. It is assumed that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} will know which of the equivalent \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} are of its interest and disregard the others.

It is important to clearly state the {\itshape ownership} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, which is that they are directly defined either in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, or in any of its inner Blocks (recursively). This has important consequences, related to the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} defined in a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may contain \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does not own. Conversely, some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be active in a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} that is not owned by the block. The intended semantic of these cases are\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item If a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} defined in a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} contains a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does not own, at the time when the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is solved they have to be treated as {\itshape constants}, with the value that they currently have. Note that this is a specific case where the data of the corresponding mathematical problem changes, but there is no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} that signals this to any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This means that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} needing to know about this will have to \char`\"{}manually\char`\"{} check whether or not a change occurred.
\item A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} not owned by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is irrelevant to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} even if it contains \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} owned by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}; it has to be thoroughly ignored. Paradoxically, such a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} may contain {\itshape only} Variables owned by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and therefore logically belonging to it, but still it has to be ignored.
\item All, and only, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} owned by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} have to be taken into account when defining what a feasible solution is. All, and only, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} owned by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be changed by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}.
\end{DoxyItemize}

It is now necessary to comment on the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}. First of all, having only one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} would seem to prevent a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} from representing a multi-\/objective optimization problem. However this is not true, as the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class (and the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} class) make no stipulations about the return value of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}. By having the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} to produce, sat, a vector of reals (or any other complex data structure with a partial ordering) rather than a single one, it is easy to represent multi-\/objective optimization problems. Of course this means that the concept of \char`\"{}optimal
solution\char`\"{} becomes that of \char`\"{}\+Pareto-\/optimal solutions\char`\"{}. This should not be much of an issue as \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} are well equipped already for producing multiple (approximate) solutions, since this is anyway useful even for single-\/objective optimization, although admittedly how the Pareto-\/optimal solutions (which may be infinitely many) are produced is still not clearly stipulated by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} interface. Perhaps a M\+O\+Solver class will be needed.

Since a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has a set of inner Blocks, each of which has its own \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, it actually has \char`\"{}many\char`\"{} objectives. It is therefore crucial to define how the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the inner Blocks \char`\"{}contribute\char`\"{} to defining the \char`\"{}total\char`\"{} objective of the problem represented by the whole \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (the father one plus the inner Blocks, recursively). The principle is simple\+: the total objective of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is given by the {\itshape sum} of its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} and of all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the inner Blocks (recursively).

This principle need some commenting. First of all, the sum may be considered just one of the very many composition operations that may conceivably be used to define the objective function of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} in terms of the objective functions of its inner Blocks. However, it is in principle always possible to keep as the objective function of each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} only the terms of the \char`\"{}total\char`\"{} objective function that only depend on variables of the given inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}; if there is none, the objective function of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be set as null (constantly 0). This indeed makes good sense, as if the objective function contains a term (say) f( x , y ) where x belongs to one inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and y to a different one, then it is more natural (although not strictly necessary) to define it in the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} rather than in either one of the inner Blocks. In fact, it is natural (although not strictly necessary) that each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} only defines its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} in terms of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} owned by the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} itself, leaving any \char`\"{}linking term\char`\"{} (be them linking \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} or terms in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} not owned by the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}) to be defined in the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This argument may be countered by saying that in some cases it may be useful to move these terms inside the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, but this is not really an issue due to the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can always \char`\"{}reformulate itself\char`\"{} so as to move around any linking terms to the place that any specific algorithm requires; see the discussion about the R3 Blocks below. Hence, assuming the sum as the default (only) composition is very natural and does not prevent using any arbitrarily complex overall objective function.

A further detail need explicit discussion, though. An \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, besides the actual function, also has a sense. This means that there are actually two rather different cases\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and that of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} have {\itshape the same} sense (say, min-\/min);
\item the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and that of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} have {\itshape different} senses (say, min-\/max).
\end{DoxyItemize}

The first case is completely obvious\+: the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is \char`\"{}just a part of
the father Block\char`\"{}. This can be stated as follows\+: if one removes everything from the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and moves it to the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, the described problem remains exactly the same.

The second case is rather different, though. On the outset, all previous definitions remain valid\+: each \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} in the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is owned by the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (which means that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can change it as it sees fit), and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is one term of that of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. However, being this a (say) min-\/max setting, this means that for any solution (considering all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} owned by the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which include those owned by the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}) to be considered optimal, {\itshape the variables of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} must be maximizing its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}}. Note that the value of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} is still comprised in that of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which is instead minimized. This allows to naturally define classical min-\/max settings, such as \[ min { yb + max { ( c - yA ) x : x \in X } : y \in Y } \] that should be familiar to many users. However, it is important to remark that in this case removing everything from the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and moving it to the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does {\itshape not} define an equivalent problem, as it is immediately seen by considering the difference between the above and \[ min { yb + ( c - yA ) x : x \in X , y \in Y } \] An apparently unfortunate consequence of this choice is that, while neatly handling min-\/max settings, it would seem to entirely prevent a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} representing multi-\/level optimization problems, where inner Blocks may be (in the multi-\/level parlance) \char`\"{}followers\char`\"{}, and therefore have to optimize a completely unrelated objective function from that of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This is not true, however. In particular, multi-\/level optimization can be made possible by defining a constraint like Optimal\+W\+R\+T( g ), where g is a (possibly, vector-\/valued) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} (and, therefore, includes an optimization sense). For an an inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} having such a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, the only feasible solutions are those that optimize w.\+r.\+t. the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} g. Hence, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can still be used to define a part of the \char`\"{}total\char`\"{} objective function, with Optimal\+W\+R\+T( g ) defining the \char`\"{}follower objective function\char`\"{}.

Another very important, feature of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is that it explicitly supports the notion that a model may need to be modified for algorithmic purposes. There are, on the outset, three different kinds of modified Blocks that are typically useful\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item A Reformulation, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that encodes a problem whose optimal solutions are optimal also for the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, while being for some reason \char`\"{}more convenient to solve\char`\"{} by some specific algorithm. Note that Reformulations may be defined over a completely different space of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, provided that some appropriate mapping can be defined between the original and reformulated space. The mapping need not be algebraic, but must obviously be algorithmic.
\item A Relaxation, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} whose optimal value provides a valid global lower bound (for a minimization problem, upper bound for a maximization one) on the optimal value of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This is typically obtained by having a larger feasible region (e.\+g., relaxing some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}) and/or an \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} whose value is smaller than the original one (for a minimization problem, larger for a maximization one) on the original feasible region. One also expects that the Relaxation is easier to solve than the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Usually, a(n algorithmic) map between solutions of the Relaxation and those of the original problem is also available, although solutions of the Relaxation may clearly not be feasible for the original problem (but they may as well be; in this case, if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} value also coincides, such a solution is actually optimal for the original problem).
\item A Restriction, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} whose optimal value provides a valid global upper bound (for a minimization problem, lower bound for a maximization one) on the optimal value of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This is typically obtained by having a smaller feasible region (e.\+g., adding some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, as in fixing some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}) and/or an \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} whose value is larger than the original one (for a minimization problem, smaller for a maximization one) on the original feasible region. One also expects that the Restriction is easier to solve than the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Again, a(n algorithmic) map between solutions of the Restriction and those of the original problem is also usually available, and these solutions are most often feasible for the original problem.
\end{DoxyItemize}

These are called \char`\"{}\+R3 Blocks\char`\"{} of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. The set of R3 Blocks of a given derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is defined by the derived class itself; the base class provides no general R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. However, due to the basic design decision about \char`\"{}names\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}), it is not in general possible to \char`\"{}copy Variable\char`\"{}. Therefore, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides support from the fact that an original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can map back solution information produced by one of its R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and, vice-\/versa, map the solution information currently stored in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to one of its R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. These operations are, again, specific for each derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and its R3 Blocks of its; the base class provides no general mechanism for this (besides the interface). Also, since the mapping between the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and one of its R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be arbitrarily complex, any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} occurring to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may require a rather complex set of different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to achieve the same \char`\"{}logical\char`\"{} effect on the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and vice-\/versa. Therefore, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class defines a general interface for a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to \char`\"{}apply the equivalent to a given Modification of
its to a R3 Block\char`\"{}, or \char`\"{}map a Modification occurring in a R3 Block to a
set of Modification for the original Block\char`\"{}. The overall R3 mechanism should be able to support a very large class of reformulation techniques.

Finally, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class also supports a number of minor but still relevant needs\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item saving current solution information, comprised dual information if available, to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_solution_8h}{Solution.\+h}}\mbox{]} and reading it back;
\item changing all the relevant parameters governing the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} behavious in one blow by means of a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} object;
\item changing all the attached \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} and their algorithmic configurations in one blow by means of a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} object;
\item printing the model in a human-\/readable form;
\item to help in the above, allow \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} to be given human-\/meaningful \char`\"{}names\char`\"{};
\item providing basic information (assumed constant throughout the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}) about the acceptable thresholds in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} violation. 
\end{DoxyItemize}

\subsection{Member Enumeration Documentation}
\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_aae1eff6b4e72eef08cf2d8334500f9b6}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_aae1eff6b4e72eef08cf2d8334500f9b6}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!verbosity\+\_\+type@{verbosity\+\_\+type}}
\index{verbosity\+\_\+type@{verbosity\+\_\+type}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{verbosity\+\_\+type}{verbosity\_type}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily enum \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_aae1eff6b4e72eef08cf2d8334500f9b6}{verbosity\+\_\+type}}}

Destructor of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}\+: it invokes \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a40a2bbe11fbdf470e474f6638fefa66f}{set\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config()}} to clean up the configuration of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to it (and recursively for its sub-\/\+Block), hence if there is any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} that has to survive the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} it has to be manually unregistered from the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} before the latter is deleted. {\footnotesize\ttfamily void add\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{std\+::list$<$ Const $>$ \&}]{list,  }\item[{std\+::list$<$ Const $>$ \&}]{newlist,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}}}]{issue\+Mod = {\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4a21b14b26b1c0093a0905f6fc010bad29}{e\+Mod\+Blck}}} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



adds a bunch of new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} at the end of the given list 

Adds a bunch of new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} at the end of the given list.

The parameter list is obviously not \char`\"{}const\char`\"{}, as the list will be updated. Note that the base class implementation of this method just does this and possibly issues the appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_mod_a_d}{Block\+Mod\+AD}}; as list is supposed to be a part of the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{}, this means that the \char`\"{}physical
representation\char`\"{} of the corresponding dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (if any exists) is not updated, as this should clearly be responsibility of the derived class (maybe within the corresponding implementation of this method).

Note that the new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} must already be organized in a list, which is \char`\"{}spliced\char`\"{} into the given one. This is crucial because splicing is the only way in which elements can be added to a list without being copied, i.\+e., their memory address being changed.

The parameter issue\+Mod decides if and how the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_mod_a_d}{Block\+Mod\+AD}} is issued, as described in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a2d499ae38e5e95cf66cd71da32b0694a}{Observer\+::make\+\_\+par()}}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a3fabbed976e93330b7f4af1bfb467670}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a3fabbed976e93330b7f4af1bfb467670}}

adds a bunch of new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} $\ast$ at the end of the given list 

Adds a bunch of pointers to new Constraints at the end of the given list.

Same as add\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints( std\+::list$<$\+Const$>$ \&list , ... ), except the arguments are lists of pointers rather than lists of objects. For most aspects, see the comments of that method.

Note that the new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} must already be organized in a list, which is \char`\"{}spliced\char`\"{} into the given one. This would not be necessary for this pointer version, because copying a pointer to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} is always OK, but it is done to keep consistence with the non-\/pointer version. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae04d4ed8bfdbc7065d5296740dd0952b}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae04d4ed8bfdbc7065d5296740dd0952b}}

mod is a smart pointer to an object of class (derived from) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} (std\+::shared\+\_\+ptr$<$\+Modification$>$, sp\+\_\+\+Mod in \mbox{\hyperlink{_modification_8h}{Modification.\+h}}), that has been created by some object (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_function}{Function}}, ...) and it is passed to this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}}. Ultimately the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} will have to reach a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} for being dispatched to all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to it, as well as to all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to its ancestors (the father, the father of the father, ...). Some Modification-\/spewing objects (typically, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_function}{Function}}) may not be directly observed by a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} but by something else (typically, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_f_row_constraint}{F\+Row\+Constraint}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_f_real_objective}{F\+Real\+Objective}}), which however ultimately belongs to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}; if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} is not a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, it is its responsibility that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object is ultimately dispatched to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Indeed, \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may also use \char`\"{}abstract\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to update their \char`\"{}physical
representation\char`\"{} to keep it in synch with their \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification_a22fe0cccfc755cfd89b240587d1f1cad}{Modification\+::concerns\+\_\+\+Block()}}\mbox{]}. Furthermore, note that a non-\/\+Block \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} may need to \char`\"{}translate\char`\"{} the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object into one or more different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} objects, according to the effect that the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} (say, to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_function}{Function}}) has on the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} (say, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_f_row_constraint}{F\+Row\+Constraint}}).

Since what is passed to this method is a shared pointer, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object is automatically destroyed after that all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} have deleted it (or, if a non-\/\+Block \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} translates it into a new set of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}, immediately after that). Note that the preferred way of making such an object is \begin{DoxyVerb}sp_Mod mod = std::make_shared<DerivedModification>( <params> );
<Observer>.add_Modification( mod );
\end{DoxyVerb}


where $<$params$>$ are the parameters of the constructor of Derived\+Modification that should be called\+: using std\+::make\+\_\+shared$<$$>$ is slightly faster then doing \char`\"{}new Derived\+Modification( $<$params$>$ )\char`\"{} and then converting the returned ordinary pointer to a smart one. Note that \char`\"{}mod\char`\"{} is a local variable in the function/method where the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} is issued, so it will likely be destroyed shortly after the call to $<$\+Observer$>$.add\+\_\+\+Modification( mod ); this is OK, because copies of the smart pointer (say, in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to which $<$\+Observer$>$ belongs, with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} == $<$\+Observer$>$ a definite possibility, and its ancestors, if any) will have been made by then. Hence, destroying \char`\"{}mod\char`\"{} will only decrease the counter by 1, effectively deleting \char`\"{}mod\char`\"{} if and only if there are no interested \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}, which is the intended semantic.

Also, note that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} (or, possibly, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} itself if it has to \char`\"{}translate\char`\"{} it) will have to downcast the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object to an actual Derived\+Modification in order to gather the information about what has been modified. These being smart pointers this cannot be achieved with the ordinary dynamic\+\_\+cast$<$$>$; rather, \begin{DoxyVerb}auto dmod = dynamic_pointer_cast<DerivedModification>( mod );
\end{DoxyVerb}


has to be used. Note that one can check that the dynamic cast worked as for an ordinary pointer, i.\+e., with \begin{DoxyVerb}dmod != nullptr       or        ! dmod
\end{DoxyVerb}


(thanks to shared\+\_\+ptr operator bool).

It may be helpful to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}}, and to \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} having to \char`\"{}intercept\char`\"{} some \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{}, that sets of \char`\"{}logically related Modification\char`\"{} be dispatched together. For this reason, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}} supports the notion that set of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} can be bunched together into a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} object. This is done by defining different \char`\"{}channels\char`\"{} where \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} can be sent, which are opened with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6459343d63aec3d39ece1e20467db47b}{open\+\_\+channel()}}. The parameter chnl specifies to which of the currently \char`\"{}open\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} objects mod has to be appended, with the following format\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item If chnl is the name of a currently open channel, which in particular implies that it is != 0, then mod is added to the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} object (possibly \char`\"{}nested inside it\char`\"{}, see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6459343d63aec3d39ece1e20467db47b}{open\+\_\+channel()}} for details).
\item If chnl is not the name of a currently open channel, (say, it is 0 as in the default), then mod is \char`\"{}sent to the default channel\char`\"{}. Unless this is changed with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae1e61f1d3f7185b6824f02488901a0ad}{set\+\_\+default\+\_\+channel()}}, this means that mod is not added to any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}, and just immediately sent along to the interested \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} / \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (if any). 
\end{DoxyItemize}

Implements \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a4ac2234c8026ccbe13860a33fc4ef14e}{Observer}}.



Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a2d7dffba0c9fd17de24549e0f1a4c760}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\index{add\+\_\+to\+\_\+\+Block\+Config@{add\+\_\+to\+\_\+\+Block\+Config}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{add\+\_\+to\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}{add\_to\_BlockConfig()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void add\+\_\+to\+\_\+\+Block\+Config (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} $\ast$}]{a\+BC,  }\item[{const bool}]{safe = {\ttfamily true} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [virtual]}}



incrementally changing information in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} 

A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is a complicated object; in general, one may want to just \char`\"{}change a few bits of it\char`\"{} rather than setting an entirely new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}. This is what this method is for.

The trick is that the provided \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} a\+BC is interpreted \char`\"{}in a
differential sense\char`\"{}. This means that every field of a\+BC that is \char`\"{}void\char`\"{} (either nullptr or empty) is interpreted as \char`\"{}leave the current value as it
is\char`\"{}, whereas a nonempty value causes the current value in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} to be replaced with the one in a\+BC. Note that when pointers to object are involved, the previously pointed object is deleted and replaced with the one in a\+BC, i.\+e., those parts of a\+BC become \char`\"{}property\char`\"{} of the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Indeed, the a\+BC object is \char`\"{}consumed\char`\"{} by this method; at the end, some parts of a\+BC will have become part of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}, and all the rest is safely disposed of. Note that if a\+BC (or one of its sub-\/\+Block\+Config) is going to replace a \char`\"{}void\char`\"{} (nullptr) (sub-\/)\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}, then this is simple because \char`\"{}adding to void is
equivalent to substituting\char`\"{}\+: a non-\/existing \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is equivalent to one having all nullptr fields, and therefore adding a\+BC (...) to a \char`\"{}void\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is the same as set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config( a\+B\+C ). In this case, the whole a\+BC becomes property of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and nothing part it is disposed of. Hence, this has the same problem as \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, i.\+e., that if the father of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (if any) has a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}, then it should be updated to reflect the addition of a\+BC. This is why this method also has the \char`\"{}safe\char`\"{} parameter, although note that a call is surely safe if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is non-\/nullptr. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab59584c81cf3770593fcf1b6371bafff}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab59584c81cf3770593fcf1b6371bafff}}

Returns true if there is any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} \char`\"{}listening to this Block\char`\"{}, which means either registered to this Bock or registered to any ancestor (father, father of father, ...) of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

This method has to be called from the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to inform each of his sons whether or not there is someone \char`\"{}listening to him\char`\"{}, and therefore to them. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae6ecad455eb1f431127411f27b5eb10f}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae6ecad455eb1f431127411f27b5eb10f}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void close\+\_\+channel (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a54ce8db639626e45b1f20c289baa989b}{Chnl\+Name}}}]{chnl }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [override]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



\char`\"{}close\char`\"{} a channel 

This method allows to \char`\"{}finalize\char`\"{} the set of \char`\"{}logically related
\+Modification\char`\"{} contained in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} associated with the channel chnl. Whatever the \char`\"{}state\char`\"{} of the channel, i.\+e., whether or not the channel is in \char`\"{}root mode\char`\"{} (currently adding to the outermost \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} of the channel, as opposed to some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} inside another \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}), the \char`\"{}outermost\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} is finally shipped to the interested \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This also closes the channel, i.\+e., chnl is no longer the name of an open channel until it is produced again by a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6459343d63aec3d39ece1e20467db47b}{open\+\_\+channel()}}.

Closing a non-\/open channel is an error and should throw exception.
