\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{deserialize()}{deserialize()}\hspace{0.1cm}{\footnotesize\ttfamily [1/3]}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}$\ast$ deserialize (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const char $\ast$}]{filename }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}



de-\/serialize a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of a net\+C\+DF file 

Top-\/level de-\/serialization method\+: takes the filename of a S\+M\+S++ net\+C\+DF file, opens it in a net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+File object, and returns the complete \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} object whose description is {\itshape the first one} found in the file. It does so by just forwarding to deserialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+File ). If something goes wrong with the entire operation, nullptr is returned. See deserialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+File ) for details of the S\+M\+S++ net\+C\+DF file format.

Note that the method is static, hence it is to be called as \begin{DoxyVerb}  Block *myBlock = Block::deserialize( somefile );
\end{DoxyVerb}


i.\+e., without any reference to any specific \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and, therefore, it can be used to construct the very first \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} if needed).

Note that the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} returned my this method is clearly not \char`\"{}empty\char`\"{}, as opposed as \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} fresh out of the factory (see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a0fb67cbfab69b75e3bc6ad92d581bd2e}{new\+\_\+\+Block()}}), but is it \char`\"{}un-\/configured\char`\"{}\+: the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} is not constructed (unless the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does this by its own volition), both the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} are not set, and (therefore) there are no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afb0cada3cc11e452ec6da432cedc8906}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afb0cada3cc11e452ec6da432cedc8906}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!deserialize@{deserialize}}
\index{deserialize@{deserialize}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{deserialize()}{deserialize()}\hspace{0.1cm}{\footnotesize\ttfamily [2/3]}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}$\ast$ deserialize (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+File \&\&}]{f,  }\item[{const unsigned int}]{idx = {\ttfamily 0} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}



de-\/serialize a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of an open net\+C\+DF S\+M\+S++ file 

Second-\/level de-\/serialization method\+: takes an open net\+C\+DF file and the index of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} into the file, and returns the correspinding complete \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} object.

There are three types of S\+M\+S++ net\+C\+DF files, corresponding to three values of the enum smspp\+\_\+net\+C\+D\+F\+\_\+file\+\_\+type \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}}\mbox{]}. Each file, when opened in a net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+File (which is also a net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group), must have an int net\+C\+DF attribute \char`\"{}\+S\+M\+S++\+\_\+file\+\_\+type\char`\"{} with one of the three values of the enum. The structure of the corresponding files is\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item e\+Prob\+File\+: the file (which is also a group) has any number of child groups with names \char`\"{}\+Prob\+\_\+0\char`\"{}, \char`\"{}\+Prob\+\_\+1\char`\"{}, ... In turn, each child group has exactly three child groups with names \char`\"{}\+Block\char`\"{}, \char`\"{}\+Block\+Config\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}\+Block\+Solver\char`\"{}, respectively. The first is intended to contain the serialization of a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, the second the serialization of a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} of the same \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and the third the serialization of a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} of the same \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, although any of the three can in principle be empty. If any of the child is not empty, it must necessarily contain a string attribute \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} contaiming the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a738e9dfd6e00507da219e4bd5a3f6be9}{name()}} of the corresponding \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} / \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class, plus od course all the information necessary to reconstruct the specific instance. Note that inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} (if any) are assumed each to be contained into a child of the group containing the original \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} / \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, recursively.
\item e\+Block\+File\+: the file (which is also a group) has any number of child groups with names \char`\"{}\+Block\+\_\+0\char`\"{}, \char`\"{}\+Block\+\_\+1\char`\"{}, ... Each child group contains the serialization of a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (the string attribute \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} and all the rest).
\item e\+Config\+File\+: the file (which is also a group) has any number of child groups with names \char`\"{}\+Config\+\_\+0\char`\"{}, \char`\"{}\+Config\+\_\+1\char`\"{}, ... Each child group contains the serialization of a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} (the string attribute \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} and all the rest).
\end{DoxyItemize}

The \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} extracted from the file is specified by the parameter idx\+: for an e\+Prob\+File it is extracted out of the net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group \char`\"{}\+Prob\+\_\+$<$idx$>$\char`\"{}, while for an e\+Block\+File it is extracted out of the net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group \char`\"{}\+Block\+\_\+$<$idx$>$\char`\"{}. Note that a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} cannot be de-\/serialized out of a e\+Config\+File. Trying to do that, as well as if something goes wrong with the entire operation (the file is not there, the \char`\"{}\+S\+M\+S++\+\_\+file\+\_\+type\char`\"{} attribute is not there, there is no required \char`\"{}\+Prob\+\_\+$<$idx$>$\char`\"{} or \char`\"{}\+Block\+\_\+$<$idx$>$\char`\"{} child group, there is any fatal error during the process, ...) results in nullptr being returned.

Note that the method is static, hence it is to be called as \begin{DoxyVerb}  Block *myBlock = Block::deserialize( netCDFfile );
\end{DoxyVerb}


i.\+e., without any reference to any specific \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and, therefore, it can be used to construct the very first \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} if needed).

What this method does is finding the right child group and forward to new\+\_\+\+Block( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group ). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4b98fbc561cffa3e92a2690c831ab3c9}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4b98fbc561cffa3e92a2690c831ab3c9}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!deserialize@{deserialize}}
\index{deserialize@{deserialize}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{deserialize()}{deserialize()}\hspace{0.1cm}{\footnotesize\ttfamily [3/3]}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void deserialize (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group \&\&}]{group,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{father = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



de-\/serialize the current \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group 

Fourth and final level de-\/serialization method\+: takes a net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group supposedly containing all the information required to de-\/serialize the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} must, starting with the \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} attribute that has to contain the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a738e9dfd6e00507da219e4bd5a3f6be9}{name()}} of the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and exception should clearly be thrown is this does not happen), and initialize the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of it. \begin{DoxyVerb} THIS IS THE METHOD TO BE IMPLEMENTED BY DERIVED CLASSES
\end{DoxyVerb}


and in fact it is virtual. The format of the information is clearly that set by the serialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group ) method of the specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class, and exception should be thrown if anything goes wrong in the process.

Two important notes need be done\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4210de3b3b076cf4273eaee2b916da84}{deserialize()}} S\+H\+O\+U\+LD O\+N\+LY BE C\+A\+L\+L\+ED ON AN E\+M\+P\+TY \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} .

A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is not supposed to handle the transition between having been previously loaded with some instance data, and then having to clean-\/up everything in order to be re-\/filled with something entirely different. If this need be done, it is cleaner to destroy the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and construct an entirely new one. A non-\/empty \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may well decide to throw exception if this is done (although it may not). As a consequence, \begin{DoxyVerb} deserialize() IS NOT EXPECTED TO ISSUE Modification ,
\end{DoxyVerb}


as it is not expected that any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} is attached to an empty \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.
\item A\+F\+T\+ER \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4210de3b3b076cf4273eaee2b916da84}{deserialize()}} T\+HE \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} IS U\+N-\/\+C\+O\+N\+F\+I\+G\+U\+R\+ED

Although clearly not \char`\"{}empty\char`\"{}, as opposed as \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} fresh out of the factory (see new\+\_\+\+Block( string )), a freshly de-\/serialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is otherwise \char`\"{}in pristine state\char`\"{}\+: the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} is not constructed (unless the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does this by its own volition), both the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} are not set, and (therefore) there are no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached. The e\+Prob\+File S\+M\+S++ nec\+C\+DF file type is precisely provided for allowing to save {\itshape all} the information required to solve a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} itself, its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} and its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}}), but de-\/serializing the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} and applying them is still the user\textquotesingle{}s responsibility.
\end{DoxyItemize}

To tally with Block\+::serialize( net\+C\+D\+F\+::\+Nc\+Group ), the base class implementation just (checks if type and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a738e9dfd6e00507da219e4bd5a3f6be9}{name()}} match, and) scans all the child Nc\+Group of the current Nc\+Group, assumes that each one of them is a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} group and de-\/serializes a sub-\/\+Block of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of it, in left-\/to-\/right order.

Note that the method has a further \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$ argument, that can be used as in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} constructor to ensure that the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has access to all the data structures of its father it needs for correctly initializing itself (if any). Indeed, in the base class implementation (that provides a clean blueprint of how this can be done by specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}), the parameter is used to pass \char`\"{}this\char`\"{} to all the sub-\/\+Block. This should be the preferred way to access to such information (provided that, as it probably should, the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has correctly initialized itself before doing its sub-\/\+Block), although note that the sub-\/\+Block can alternatively access the information about the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} by peeking into the \char`\"{}father\char`\"{} Nc\+Group via Nc\+Group\+::get\+Parent\+Group(). 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a817e3ce47ea40c14517ec16c6b62b814}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_simple_m_i_l_p_block_a817e3ce47ea40c14517ec16c6b62b814}{Simple\+M\+I\+L\+P\+Block}}, and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_structured_m_i_l_p_block_a817e3ce47ea40c14517ec16c6b62b814}{Structured\+M\+I\+L\+P\+Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{f\+\_\+factory()}{f\_factory()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static Block\+Factory\+Map\& f\+\_\+factory (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}, {\ttfamily [protected]}}



method incapsulating the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} factory 

This method returns the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} factory, which is a static object. The rationale for using a method is that this is the \char`\"{}\+Construct On First Use
\+Idiom\char`\"{} that solves the \char`\"{}static initialization order problem\char`\"{}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints@{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints}}
\index{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints@{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}{generate\_dynamic\_constraints()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{dycc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



generate the dynamic Constraints of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

This method is intended as a hook for dynamic generation of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}. The idea is that the information available to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, e.\+g. stored in its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, may allow the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to generate more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}. This is necessary to use valid inequalities (better describing the feasible region of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} than the \char`\"{}straightforward\char`\"{} formulation can) or lazy constraints (describing the feasible region of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}) when the corresponding \char`\"{}families\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} are \char`\"{}very large\char`\"{} (say, exponentially many) and therefore can only be generated dynamically.

Note that, from a semantic viewpoint, dynamic constraints in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are thought as \char`\"{}being there even they are not there\char`\"{}; that is, they contribute to defining the feasible region of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which means that each feasible solution must satisfy them all. Only, since they can be \char`\"{}many\char`\"{} it is better not to explicitly construct all of them, but only \char`\"{}the
subset that is useful for algorithmic reasons\char`\"{}. This in particular means that if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, any call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4204ffb542117f4ea97eeceeda4da079}{is\+\_\+feasible()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]} cannot rely only on the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to produce a correct return value. Also, it is important to remark that there are two different classes of dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, \char`\"{}valid
inequalities\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}lazy constraints\char`\"{}, with a different semantic meaning. Valid inequalities potentially strengthen the formulation of the problem but do not change the set of feasible solutions; therefore, how many of them are generated (comprised none) does not change the optimal solutions of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Conversely, lazy constraints change the set of feasible solutions; therefore, lazy constraints {\itshape must} be generated (separated) before a solution is confirmed as feasible. This is, of course, true for general-\/purpose \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} relying on the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}; specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may know the feasible set without any need for dynamic lazy constraints to be ever generated.

When this method is called, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should attempt at generating new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}. If the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} has been constructed \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}}\mbox{]}, then any newly found \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} will be added to the corresponding list via a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_af8871ad704bc4e8f3109a26103bfb3ff}{add\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraint()}}, thereby triggering the appropriate (abstract) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}. This may not be necessary, since a specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may be able to work with a specialized version of the dynamic constraints (say, cutsets in an appropriate graph). In this case, a specific (physical) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} has to be issued that the specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} has to be capable to recognize, thereby ignoring the corresponding abstract modification. Note that if the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} has been also constructed, both types of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} will have to be issued, with each type of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} having to figure out which one to react to. Of course, in order to be able to construct the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of a dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} one must have already constructed the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} first, but this is almost free because \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_acd79fd0980af547f2f06241cb989094e}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} has to be called (with appropriate parameters) before \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} is.

In general, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have several different types (groups) of dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (be them valid inequalities or lazy constraints); not all of them must necessarily be generated all the time. For instance, valid inequalities may be generated on unfeasible continuous solutions, whereas lazy constraints may be generated on feasible integer ones. Also, some families of dynamic constraints may be cheaper to generate than others, and it may make algorithmic sense to give different priorities at different stages of the solution process. Furthermore, even for the same family of dynamic constraints there could be different generation (separation) procedures (say, heuristic and exact), each with different algorithmic parameters, among which possibly the time that can be spent in the process. Finally, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have several (different) sub-\/\+Block, recursively, and dynamic constraints for all sub-\/\+Block should in principle be generated whenever this method is called for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

For all these reasons, the method has the parameter dycc, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object. The actual parameter may be of any specific derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, and contain all the information that is relevant, such as which families of dynamic constraints to be separated, and which which algorithm. Possibly the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object may even contain one or more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with attached \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} to represent and solve the separation problem, so that the algorithmic parameters of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} (say, maximum time and required accuracy) can be used to control the separation process. Also, if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has sub-\/\+Block, some of which have dynamic constraints, then the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object should contain an appropriate \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object for each of these.

Note that the dycc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with dycc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the Block\+Config() is used. Indeed, the method is not pure virtual, but it is given a default implementation doing nothing but calling itself for each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with no (= default = nullptr) argument. This is correct for those \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} have no dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} at all, and for which all the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} either have the same property or are only to be called with the default configuration set by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}. Note that if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}separate all the dynamic Constraint that you have, if any\char`\"{}, which is fine if the separation process actually has no parameters (say, a single class of valid inequalities with an easy exact separation algorithm). Thus, the default implementation automatically works in the \char`\"{}simple\char`\"{} cases, while for any other case the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to implement its own version, say \char`\"{}unpacking\char`\"{} its \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object to specific sub-\/\+Configuration for each of its sub-\/\+Block. This cannot be done in the default implementation because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} dycc for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will likely have to be \char`\"{}unpacked\char`\"{}, with each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} getting its own specific sub-\/\+Configuration, but this can only be done by a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} for a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, as the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class does not have direct support for the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} contains a sub-\/\+Configuration for each sub-\/\+Block of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}}

\index{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables@{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}{generate\_dynamic\_variables()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{dyvv = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



generate the dynamic Variables of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

This method is intended as a hook for dynamic generation of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}. The idea is that the information available to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (e.\+g. stored in dual variables of its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, if they are defined) may allow the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to generate more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} that can be used to get better solutions. This is necessary for Blocks that have in principle a \char`\"{}very large\char`\"{} number of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (say, exponentially many) which therefore can only be generated dynamically.

Note that, from a semantic viewpoint, dynamic variables are considered to \char`\"{}be there even they are not there\char`\"{}. That is, it is assumed that all the dynamic variables not explicitly generated are implicitly present in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} set at their default value (most often, zero), and that this does not change the fact that the (explicitly constructed part of the) solution is feasible. Hence, since the dynamic variables can be \char`\"{}many\char`\"{}, only those that are actually necessary to encode the optimal solution need be explicitly constructed (although algorithms will typically generate more than these while iteratively searching for the best set\+: if the latter were known a-\/priori, it could be encoded as a set of static Variables). This in particular means that if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has dynamic variables, any call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a44ef42401ecae613574f3df619480de1}{is\+\_\+optimal()}} cannot rely only on the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to produce a correct return value.

When this method is called, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should attempt at generating new Variables. If the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} has been constructed \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_acd79fd0980af547f2f06241cb989094e}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}}\mbox{]}, then any newly found \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} will be added to the corresponding list via a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_af7677194bbf8a194cf2105e72a1be2e7}{add\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variable()}}, thereby triggering the appropriate (abstract) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}. This may not be necessary, since a specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may be able to work with a specialized version of the dynamic variables (say, paths in an appropriate graph). In this case, a specific (physical) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} has to be issued that the specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} has to be capable to recognize, thereby ignoring the corresponding abstract modification. Note that if the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} has been also constructed, both types of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} will have to be issued, with each type of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} having to figure out which one to react to.

Note that any new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} typically allows more feasible solutions to exist; hence, in general if dynamic Variables exist, they have necessarily to be generated (priced in) before a solution is certified to be optimal. There can be exceptions for Variables that only reformulate the set of feasible solutions without actually adding it any new element, such as those corresponding to dual-\/optimal inequalities; this is analogous to the distinction between lazy constraints and valid inequalities.

In principle, one \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have several different types of dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}; not all of them must necessarily be generated all the time. For instance, some families of dynamic Variables may be cheaper to generate (priced in) than others, and it may make algorithmic sense to give different priorities at different stages of the solution process. Furthermore, even for the same family of dynamic Variables there could be different generation (pricing) procedures (say, heuristic and exact), each with different algorithmic parameters, among which possibly the time that can be spent in the process. Finally, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have several (different) sub-\/\+Block, recursively, and dynamic variables for all sub-\/\+Block should in principle be generated whenever this method is called for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

For all these reasons, the method has the parameter dyvv, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object. The actual parameter may be of any specific derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, and contain all the information that is relevant, such as which families of dynamic variables to be priced-\/in, and which which algorithm. Possibly the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object may even contain one or more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with attached \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} to represent and solve the pricing problem, so that the algorithmic parameters of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} (say, maximum time and required accuracy) can be used to control the pricing process. Also, if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has sub-\/\+Block, some of which have dynamic variables, then the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object should contain an appropriate \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object for each of these.

Note that the dyvv parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with dycc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the Block\+Config() is used. Indeed, the method is not pure virtual, but it is given a default implementation doing nothing but calling itself for each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with no (= default = nullptr) argument. This is correct for those \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} have no dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} at all, and for which all the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} either have the same property or are only to be called with the default configuration set by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}. Note that if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}price-\/in all the dynamic Variable that you have, if
any\char`\"{}, which is fine if the pricing process actually has no parameters (say, a single class of dynamic variables with an easy exact pricing algorithm). Thus, the default implementation automatically works in the \char`\"{}simple\char`\"{} cases, while for any other case the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to implement its own version, say \char`\"{}unpacking\char`\"{} its \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object to specific sub-\/\+Configuration for each of its sub-\/\+Block. This cannot be done in the default implementation because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} dyvv for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will likely have to be \char`\"{}unpacked\char`\"{}, with each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} getting its own specific sub-\/\+Configuration, but this can only be done by a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} for a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, as the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class does not have direct support for the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} contains a sub-\/\+Configuration for each sub-\/\+Block of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae14038d64cf6067f15cf3f53fd7af9e0}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae14038d64cf6067f15cf3f53fd7af9e0}}

generate the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

This method serves is to ensure that the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is initialized, so that it can be read with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afed5fdabb9a34885aaa38c3dd4e67834}{get\+\_\+objective()}}. Of course, in order to be able to construct the \char`\"{}abstract
representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} one must have already constructed the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} first, see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_acd79fd0980af547f2f06241cb989094e}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}}.

Because a (specialized) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may not need the description of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} in terms of its \char`\"{}abstract\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, that may not actually be constructed until this method is called for the first time. A derived class can ignore all this and just construct it right away, but this may be work and memory wasted if no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} actually uses it. In general, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have any arbitrarily complex objective. Thus, like all other generate\+\_\+$\ast$(), the method has the parameter objc, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object. The actual parameter may be of any specific derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, and contain all the information that is relevant. Possibly the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object may even contain one or more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with attached \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} to represent and solve some complex (say, Lagrangian) function.

Note that the objc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with objc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the Block\+Config() is used. Indeed, the method is not pure virtual, but it is given a default implementation doing nothing but calling itself for each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with no (= default = nullptr) argument. This is correct for those \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} have no complex objective, and for which all the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} either have the same property or are only to be called with the default configuration set by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aa2b7e92a516434c33f247eef8574fb94}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

A derived class can ignore all this and just construct them right away, but this may be work and memory wasted if no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} actually uses them. Any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} should ensure that this method has been called at least once before making any attempt at using the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, unless it knows for sure that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} it is working with has done that already. Note that it is easy to check whether or not \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} still has to be called by just testing if \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afaf2dca465cd70d9d76bcfe839e52aec}{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}}.size() == 0, although this is a necessary but not sufficient condition, as a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may not have any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} at all.

In general, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can have several different types (groups) of static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}; not all the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may require all of them, be them directly of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} or of any of its sub-\/\+Block, recursively. This is why the method has the parameter stcc, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object. The actual parameter may be of any specific derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, and contain all the information that specifies which of the types (groups) of static \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} must actually be constructed. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has sub-\/\+Block, then the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object should contain an appropriate \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object for each of these.

Note that the stcc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with stcc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is used. Indeed, the method is not pure virtual, but it is given a default implementation doing nothing but calling itself for each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with no (= default = nullptr) argument. This is correct for those \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that either have no static Constraints at all or that construct them all anyway, and for which all the inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} either have the same property or are only to be called with the default configuration set by the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}}. Note that if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}construct all the static
\+Constraint that you have, if any\char`\"{}. Thus, the default implementation automatically works in the \char`\"{}simple\char`\"{} cases, while for any other case the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to implement its own version, say \char`\"{}unpacking\char`\"{} its \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object to specific sub-\/\+Configuration for each of its sub-\/\+Block. This cannot be done in the default implementation because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} stcc for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will likely have to be \char`\"{}unpacked\char`\"{}, with each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} getting its own specific sub-\/\+Configuration, but this can only be done by a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} for a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, as the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class does not have direct support for the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} contains a sub-\/\+Configuration for each specific sub-\/\+Block of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

This method serves is to ensure that the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, be they static or dynamic, of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is initialized, so that it can be read with \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6ca8b345bb506a8ac45eef39887bebea}{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9c17adfb0c344b44e163d40f7fc6926f}{get\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]}. For the dynamic ones this may (or may not) imply that the lists are empty, with the dynamic generation being done into \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]}.

This method has to be used (rather, {\itshape not} used) with caution, because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are in general \char`\"{}thought to be always there\char`\"{}. Indeed, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are \char`\"{}the interface between the Solver and the user
of the Block\char`\"{}, since they are where the solution information is written. However, specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may have more compact/efficient ways to represent their solution information (say, in a T\+SP one could use n integers describing the permutation rather than n$^\wedge$2 binaries), which a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may exploit and that a user of that specialized \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may read through the specialized interface. Thus, if only using a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} via its specialized interface, the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be avoided.

Furthermore, not all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may be \char`\"{}equally important\char`\"{}. For instance, only a (maybe, quite small) subset of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} may be required for certain uses of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which however logically also has a (much larger) set of \char`\"{}auxiliary\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}. If the latter are not needed by any of the Solver/users interacting with the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, it is possible to avoid to construct them at all. This is somehow related to, albeit different from, the fact that certain \char`\"{}types\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} may be \char`\"{}many\char`\"{}, and therefore necessarily have to be generated dynamicallt, see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} below.

Note, however, that all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} appearing in any (\char`\"{}abstract
representation\char`\"{} of a) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} that is explicitly constructed \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} above\mbox{]} must obviously be constructed, and this {\itshape before} the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} is. reading the {\itshape dynamic} Constraints of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Method for reading the {\itshape dynamic} Constraints of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. It returns a vector of boost\+::any, each element of which is supposed to contain only one among\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item a pointer to a single std\+::list$<$\+C$>$, where class C is derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (obviously you can\textquotesingle{}t make a std\+::list of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} class, which is why pointers to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} are also allowed, see below);
\item a pointer to a single std\+::list$<$\+C $\ast$$>$, where class C is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (List\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Const $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_constraint_8h}{Constraint.\+h}}) or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item a pointer to a std\+::vector$<$std\+::list$<$\+C$>$ $>$, where class C is derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item a pointer to a std\+::vector$<$std\+::list$<$\+C $\ast$$>$ $>$, where class C is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (Vec\+\_\+\+List\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Const $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_constraint_8h}{Constraint.\+h}}) or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$std\+::list$<$\+C$>$ , K$>$, where class C is derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, in principle with any K (but the limit for K may be dictated by \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_ga8ec1a694aa7a5fa58ab801c1ea419fa8}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+static()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_gab299af634bc1ca9be3a8d45a14560036}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+thing()}} in \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}});
\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$std\+::list$<$\+C $\ast$$>$ , K$>$, where class C is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (Vec\+\_\+\+List\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Const$<$\+K$>$ $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_constraint_8h}{Constraint.\+h}}) or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that this is the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which is why these are all pointers. It is assumed that the actual \mbox{[}vectors or multi\+\_\+array\mbox{]} of lists of \mbox{[}of pointers to\mbox{]} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} have been defined in the proper derived class, and can therefore be accessed in some model-\/specific version from their specialized interface. Anyway, they depend on the specific data that characterizes the derived class, which is responsible of the allocation and deallocation of the corresponding memory (the \char`\"{}physical representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}). These being {\itshape dynamic} Constraints, the lists can well be (but need not necessarily be) empty when the object is initialized, and be populated (and de-\/populated) dynamically during the lifetime of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. The two representations of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may, or may not, coincide\+: however, Variables and Constraints M\+U\+ST {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} BE C\+O\+P\+I\+ED BY V\+A\+L\+UE, B\+E\+C\+A\+U\+SE T\+H\+E\+IR M\+E\+M\+O\+RY A\+D\+D\+R\+E\+SS IS T\+H\+E\+IR N\+A\+ME. Hence, should a derived class choose to implement its \char`\"{}physical representation\char`\"{} as (...) lists of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, as opposed to (...) list of pointers, it has to be {\itshape very} careful to {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} M\+O\+D\+I\+FY T\+H\+E\+SE V\+E\+C\+T\+O\+RS (in particular, increase their size) during the lifetime of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} in order to avoid the risk that some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} may change their memory location, hence their \char`\"{}name\char`\"{}.

A F\+O\+R\+T\+I\+O\+RI, S\+I\+ZE OF T\+HE \mbox{[}...\mbox{]} A\+R\+R\+A\+YS W\+H\+O\+SE P\+O\+I\+N\+T\+E\+RS A\+RE P\+R\+O\+V\+I\+D\+ED BY T\+H\+IS M\+E\+T\+H\+OD M\+U\+ST {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} BE C\+H\+A\+N\+G\+ED BY W\+H\+O\+M\+E\+V\+ER R\+E\+A\+DS T\+H\+EM. This also implies that T\+HE A\+D\+D\+R\+E\+SS OF A\+LL L\+I\+S\+TS OF D\+Y\+N\+A\+M\+IC Constraints W\+I\+LL N\+E\+V\+ER C\+H\+A\+N\+GE, A\+ND T\+H\+E\+R\+E\+F\+O\+RE IT C\+AN BE U\+S\+ED AT T\+HE C\+O\+L\+L\+E\+C\+T\+I\+VE N\+A\+ME F\+OR T\+H\+AT S\+ET OF D\+Y\+N\+A\+M\+IC Constraints (each one of which will then have its individual name given by its memory address, which will also never change). Since it must, conversely, be possible to change the individual Constraints, the arrays cannot be const (a size-\/cons, contents-\/mutable array should be used, which is possible but just too complicated at this point). It would be possible to enforce this for \mbox{[}...\mbox{]} arrays of {\itshape pointers}, but since it is (currently) not done for the others it\textquotesingle{}s not worth the bother; besides, resizing a vector of pointers do not change the address of the original objects (although hell may break loose for many other reasons).

The rationale of the structure is that the lists can be indiced over (in principle) as many indices ad one wants, but each element of the list is a \mbox{[}pointer to a\mbox{]} {\itshape single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}}. If the user needs to have multi-\/dimensional dynamic Constraints (say, c\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} with a dynamic index \char`\"{}i\char`\"{} where each c\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} is a matrix of Constraints depending on two static indices \char`\"{}j\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}k\char`\"{}), then she has to put the dynamic index at the end and ensure that all the lists are updated in the same way (say, define the 2-\/dimensional array of lists of (...) Constraints c\mbox{[} j \mbox{]}\mbox{[} k \mbox{]}, and be sure that all the lists for all the indices \char`\"{}j\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}k\char`\"{} are updated simultaneously each time a new index \char`\"{}i\char`\"{} is added, or an old one is removed). Lists of lists of (...) Constraints are not supported (just make that a unique list). In other words, the size of the vectors of lists is {\itshape fixed} and must {\itshape never} be changed\+: the only thing that can change (freely) is the size of each list.

Similarly, the size of the vector of dynamic Constraints is {\itshape not} supposed to change along the life of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}\+: which {\itshape groups} of Constraints are there is \char`\"{}the structure of the Block\char`\"{}, and this is assumed to be given. Individual Constraints can indeed appear and disappear, which is what precisely dynamic Constraints are for, but \char`\"{}the set of indices of
   Constraints\char`\"{} is assumed to be given once and for all. That is, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_af8871ad704bc4e8f3109a26103bfb3ff}{add\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraint()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]} should only be called (by derived classes) during the initialization of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and never thereafter. More precisely, because some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may not need to access the Constraints at all, the idea is that Constraints (be them static or dynamic) are only generated if and when they are actually required. This is what the methods \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}} and generate\+\_\+abstract\+\_\+constraints() are about. In particular, this method can only be called if generate\+\_\+abstract\+\_\+constraints() has been called, and the latter call may trigger a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}}. Note that, once the sets of static and dynamic Constraints have been constructed, new dynamic constraints can be repeatedly generated by calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}} \mbox{[}see above\mbox{]}. However, because while the \char`\"{}shape\char`\"{} of the set of dynamic Constraints is constant, it still makes no sense to call this method more than once. If generate\+\_\+abstract\+\_\+constraints() had already been called at the time when \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}} is called, then each newly inserted \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} triggers an appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} that makes any registered \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} aware of the fact that it has been added. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9c17adfb0c344b44e163d40f7fc6926f}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a9c17adfb0c344b44e163d40f7fc6926f}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!get\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables@{get\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables}}

\item a pointer to a single std\+::list$<$\+V $\ast$$>$, where class V is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (List\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Var $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_variable_8h}{Variable.\+h}}) or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}};
\item a pointer to a std\+::vector$<$std\+::list$<$\+V$>$ $>$, where class V is derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}};
\item a pointer to a std\+::vector$<$std\+::list$<$\+V $\ast$$>$ $>$, where class V is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (Vec\+\_\+\+List\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Var $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_variable_8h}{Variable.\+h}}) or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}};
\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$std\+::list$<$\+V$>$ , K$>$, where class V is derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, in principle with any K (but the limit for K may be dictated by \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_ga8ec1a694aa7a5fa58ab801c1ea419fa8}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+static()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_gab299af634bc1ca9be3a8d45a14560036}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+thing()}} in \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}});
\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$std\+::list$<$\+V $\ast$$>$ , K$>$, where class V is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (Vec\+\_\+\+List\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Var$<$\+K$>$ $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_variable_8h}{Variable.\+h}}) or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}.
\end{DoxyItemize}

A F\+O\+R\+T\+I\+O\+RI, S\+I\+ZE OF T\+HE \mbox{[}...\mbox{]} A\+R\+R\+A\+YS W\+H\+O\+SE P\+O\+I\+N\+T\+E\+RS A\+RE P\+R\+O\+V\+I\+D\+ED BY T\+H\+IS M\+E\+T\+H\+OD M\+U\+ST {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} BE C\+H\+A\+N\+G\+ED BY W\+H\+O\+M\+E\+V\+ER R\+E\+A\+DS T\+H\+EM. This also implies that T\+HE A\+D\+D\+R\+E\+SS OF A\+LL L\+I\+S\+TS OF D\+Y\+N\+A\+M\+IC Variables W\+I\+LL N\+E\+V\+ER C\+H\+A\+N\+GE, A\+ND T\+H\+E\+R\+E\+F\+O\+RE IT C\+AN BE U\+S\+ED AT T\+HE C\+O\+L\+L\+E\+C\+T\+I\+VE N\+A\+ME F\+OR T\+H\+AT S\+ET OF D\+Y\+N\+A\+M\+IC Variables (each one of which will then have its individual name given by its memory address, which will also never change). The rationale of the structure is that the lists can be indiced over (in principle) as many indices ad one wants, but each element of the list is a \mbox{[}pointer to a\mbox{]} {\itshape single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}}. If the user needs to have multi-\/dimensional dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (say, x\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} with a dynamic index \char`\"{}i\char`\"{} where each x\mbox{[} i \mbox{]} is a matrix of Constraints depending on two static indices \char`\"{}j\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}k\char`\"{}), then she has to put the dynamic index at the end and ensure that all the lists are updated in the same way (say, define the 2-\/dimensional array of lists of (...) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} x\mbox{[} j \mbox{]}\mbox{[} k \mbox{]}, and be sure that all the lists for all the indices \char`\"{}j\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}k\char`\"{} are updated simultaneously each time a new index \char`\"{}i\char`\"{} is added, or an old one is removed). In other words, the size of the vectors of lists is {\itshape fixed} and must {\itshape never} be changed\+: the only thing that can change (freely) is the size of each list.

Similarly, the size of the vector of dynamic Variables is {\itshape not} supposed to change along the life of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}\+: which {\itshape groups} of Variables are there is \char`\"{}the structure of the Block\char`\"{}, and this is assumed to be given. Individual Variables can indeed appear and disappear, which is what precisely dynamic Variables are for, but \char`\"{}the set of indices of
    Variables\char`\"{} is assumed to be given once and for all. That is, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_af7677194bbf8a194cf2105e72a1be2e7}{add\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variable()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]} should only be called (by derived classes) during the initialization of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and never thereafter.

As opposed to Constraints, Variables necessarily need to be initialized immediately when the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is constructed. However, this does not mean that their \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} is necessarily available\+: this method can only be called if generate\+\_\+abstract\+\_\+variables() has been called. However, once the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the sets of dynamic Variables have been constructed, new dynamic variables can be repeatedly generated by calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} \mbox{[}see above\mbox{]}. . $\ast$ Note that new dynamic constraints can be repeatedly generated by calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} \mbox{[}see above\mbox{]}. However, because while the \char`\"{}shape\char`\"{} of the set of dynamic Variables is constant, it still makes no sense to call this method more than once. {\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___block___t_y_p_e_s_ga7cbe2d2777d0b63cf75c041bf7e60dff}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+\+Block}}\& get\+\_\+nested\+\_\+\+Blocks (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



reading the vector of inner sub-\/\+Blocks of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Method for reading the vector of inner sub-\/\+Blocks of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Note that the vector v\+\_\+\+Block is the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the inner Blocks. In this case it may well coincide with the \char`\"{}physical\char`\"{} one, but the point is that the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class does not claim ownership of the Blocks in the v\+\_\+\+Block field. In other words, the sub-\/\+Blocks in that vector are {\itshape not} deleted by the destructor of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class\+: whomever produced them in the first place (likely, the current derived \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class itself) must take responsibility for this. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afed5fdabb9a34885aaa38c3dd4e67834}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afed5fdabb9a34885aaa38c3dd4e67834}}

Getting the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}; the boost any contains a pointer to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} or to any class derived by \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block@{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block}}
\index{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block@{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}{get\_R3\_Block()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}$\ast$ get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{r3bc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



produces a new R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

This method is the general interface by which a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can produce a related \char`\"{}\+R3 Block\char`\"{}, i.\+e., typically\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item a Reformulation, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that encodes a problem whose optimal solutions are optimal also for the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}};
\item a Relaxation, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} whose optimal value provides valid global lower/upper bounds for the optimal value of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, assuming that was a minimization/maximization problem, while hopefully being easier to solve;
\item a Restriction, i.\+e., a different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} whose optimal value provides valid global upper/lower bounds for the optimal value of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, assuming that was a minimization/maximization problem, while hopefully being easier to solve.
\end{DoxyItemize}

The set of R3 Blocks of a given derived class from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is defined by the derived class itself; the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides no general R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. The only provision that the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class makes is that any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should allow for a very specific type of Reformulation\+: copy (producing a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that is identical to the original one). This is, however, not really enforced\+: the expectation is that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} (with default = nullptr parameter) is equivalent to a typical clone() method, but there is nothing really forcing derived classes to abide to this provision.

Note that, due to the basic design decision about the \char`\"{}names\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, it is not in general possible to \char`\"{}copy Variable\char`\"{}. Hence, the newly produced \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} -- even if just a straight copy -- will have different \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} than the original one. Using solution information from the new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to (help) solv(e/ing) the old one, or vice-\/versa, requires being able to map the new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} into the old ones, or vice-\/versa. This is something that specific derived classes, having implemented the R3 transformation, can efficiently do, and therefore it is demanded to them \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}} below\mbox{]}; the base class provides no general mechanism for this, besides the interface.

Each derived class can produce, in principle, any number of different R3 Blocks; the parameter r3bc is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object which allows to tell which one has to be produced. Of course, the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with sub-\/\+Block may require each sub-\/\+Block to produce R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of its (recursively), hence the use of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} that allows arbitrarily complex nested parameters to be used.

A specific point to be clarified concerns dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}. In principle, dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are thought as \char`\"{}being
there even they are not there\char`\"{}; that is, they contribute to defining the feasible region of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which means that each feasible solution must satisfy them all. Only, since they can be \char`\"{}many\char`\"{} not all of them are explicitly constructed, but only \char`\"{}the subset that is useful for
algorithmic reasons\char`\"{}. Similarly, dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} are \char`\"{}there even they are
not there\char`\"{}\+: all dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} not explicitly generated are assumed to be there in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} set at their default value (most often, zero), and it is assumed that this does not change the fact that the (explicitly constructed part of the) solution is feasible. Hence, since the dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} can be \char`\"{}many\char`\"{}, only those that are actually necessary to encode the optimal solution need be explicitly constructed.

When an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is constructed, say a copy, two different cases may occur\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} inherits \char`\"{}all\char`\"{} the dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} and/or \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}};
\item the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} only inherits the dynamic Constraints and/or \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} that have been explicitly constructed in the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} at the time when the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is constructed.
\end{DoxyItemize}

The difference lies in the fact that the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may be \char`\"{}generic\char`\"{}, i.\+e., constructed to only work with the abstract implementation of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. In this case, it cannot possibly really \char`\"{}know\char`\"{} all the dynamic Variable/\+Constraint. In other words, generating Variable/\+Constraint may require complex separation/pricing procedures\+: these are surely available to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, but may not be so to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. If they are, then the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may generate Variable/\+Constraint independently from the original one\+: these can then \char`\"{}imported back\char`\"{} by the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} with appropriate methods \mbox{[}see map\+\_\+back\+\_\+modification()\mbox{]}. If they are not, dynamic Variable/\+Constraint can only be generated by the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}; they may possibly be added to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} later on \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a26eeed482e287e1f8a384826c789f9e0}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification()}}\mbox{]}, if the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} supports it.

The method is given an extremely lazy default implementation refusing to produce any kind of R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, comprised the \char`\"{}copy\char`\"{} one. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a36fbb6f247e14792e84f68e801c49783}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.
