\index{get\+\_\+\+Solution@{get\+\_\+\+Solution}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{get\+\_\+\+Solution()}{get\_Solution()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}}$\ast$ get\+\_\+\+Solution (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{solc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{bool}]{emptys = {\ttfamily true} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



returns a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} representing the current solution of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

This method must construct and return a (pointer to a) \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object representing the current \char`\"{}solution state\char`\"{} of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. A \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} would typically store the values of the static and dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, as well as the values of the dual variables of the Constraints if any, although it can in principle do it in whatever format and without explicit reference to the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and Constraints, which may not have been constructed. Indeed, the returned object will clearly not be of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} class, but of an appropriate derived class, either specialized for the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} at hand, or at least \char`\"{}compatible\char`\"{} with it (e.\+g., only using the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, which in this case must then clearly have been constructed).

This method supports the general notion that \char`\"{}not all the solution might
be required\char`\"{}, i.\+e., that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object may only store partial (dual) solution information corresponding to only some subset of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}). Because, as usual, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can itself have multiple \char`\"{}sets\char`\"{} of Variable/\+Constraint, and also have sub-\/\+Block (recursively) each of which can in turn have many ones, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object is required to be able to specify any arbitrarily complex subset of the solution information. Note that the specified subset of the solution information is \char`\"{}baked in\char`\"{} the returned \+::\+Solution object, which will therefore for all its life only read/write that part of the overall solution information.

As usual, the solc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which is shared between this method, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}}. That is, if the method is called with solc = nullptr then the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} from the Block\+Config() is used. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}save all solution
information\char`\"{}. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} also has dual information attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, this should be taken to mean \char`\"{}copy all of both solution and
dual information\char`\"{}.

Once constructed, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object can read the (corresponding subset of) solution information from the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that has created any number of times. Note, however, that (unless explicitly declared otherwise by the specific \+::\+Solution class), a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object can only read/write solution information from {\itshape the very same} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that created it, i.\+e., not even from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of the same derived class. Since doing the copying may have a nontrivial cost, the second parameter emptys specifies (if false) that the returned \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object must be already \char`\"{}loaded\char`\"{} with the current (subset of) solution information of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, or (if true) that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} object will be \char`\"{}empty\char`\"{} (un-\/initialized), so that it will have to read() itself from the object before being significant.

The default implementation of the method works for \char`\"{}extremely lazy\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that does not ever want to save any solution information, and/or relies to \char`\"{}general-\/purpose\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solution}{Solution}} objects that only use the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} to work. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a55335e0e301e1bfa9592e8532bfd61e7}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a412a4ff2922e8d95f80589b1f6522d77}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a412a4ff2922e8d95f80589b1f6522d77}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!get\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config@{get\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config}}
\index{get\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config@{get\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{get\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config()}{get\_SolverConfig()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}}$\ast$ get\+\_\+\+Solver\+Config (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} $\ast$}]{svcc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [virtual]}}



getting the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

$<$ This method gets information about the current set of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and its sub-\/\+Block, recursively) under the form of a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} object.

If a non-\/null svcc is provided, then the pointed object (that is assumed to be \char`\"{}empty\char`\"{}) is \char`\"{}filled\char`\"{} with the data of the current configuration, and returned. Otherwise, a new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} object is created, filled and returned. This is done to allow \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to return objects of a class derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} containing information about specific parts of their \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} configuration that are not present in the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_solver_config}{Block\+Solver\+Config}} class, filling only that part and then using method of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class to fill-\/in the standard part. However, the \char`\"{}final
user\char`\"{} should not bother and assume that the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will ultimately produce the right kind of object, thereby leaving the parameter to its nullptr default value. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afaf2dca465cd70d9d76bcfe839e52aec}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_afaf2dca465cd70d9d76bcfe839e52aec}}

\index{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints@{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}{get\_static\_constraints()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___s_m_s__types_gaa16aeddc7a50d64299ee0f8d9ca5dc7d}{c\+\_\+\+Vec\+\_\+any}}\& get\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}}



reading the {\itshape static} Constraints of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Method for reading the {\itshape static} Constraints of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. It returns a vector of boost\+::any, each element of which is supposed to contain only one among\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item a pointer to a single \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (p\+\_\+\+Const in \mbox{\hyperlink{_constraint_8h}{Constraint.\+h}}) or to any class derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item a pointer to a std\+::vector of any class derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (obviously you can\textquotesingle{}t make a std\+::vector of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} class, which is why pointers to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} are also allowed, see below);
\item a pointer to a std\+::vector of pointers to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (Vec\+\_\+p\+\_\+\+Const $\ast$ in \mbox{\hyperlink{_constraint_8h}{Constraint.\+h}}) or any class derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}};
\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$\+C , K$>$, where C is any class derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} (obviously you can\textquotesingle{}t make a multi\+\_\+array of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} class), in principle with any K (but the limit for K may be dictated by \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_ga8ec1a694aa7a5fa58ab801c1ea419fa8}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+static()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_gab299af634bc1ca9be3a8d45a14560036}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+thing()}} in \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}});
\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$\+C $\ast$ , K$>$, i.\+e., of pointers of any class C that is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, in principle with any K (but the limit for K may be dictated by \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_ga8ec1a694aa7a5fa58ab801c1ea419fa8}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+static()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_gab299af634bc1ca9be3a8d45a14560036}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+thing()}} in \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}});
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that this is the \char`\"{}abstract representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, which is why these are all pointers. It is assumed that the actual \mbox{[}vectors or multi\+\_\+array\mbox{]} \mbox{[}of pointers to\mbox{]} Constraints have been defined in the proper derived class, and can therefore be accessed in some model-\/specific version from their specialized interface. Anyway, they depend on the specific data that characterizes the derived class, which is responsible of the allocation and deallocation of the corresponding memory (the \char`\"{}physical representation\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}). The two representations of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may, or may not, coincide\+: however, Variables and Constraints M\+U\+ST {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} BE C\+O\+P\+I\+ED BY V\+A\+L\+UE, B\+E\+C\+A\+U\+SE T\+H\+E\+IR M\+E\+M\+O\+RY A\+D\+D\+R\+E\+SS IS T\+H\+E\+IR N\+A\+ME. Hence, should a derived class choose to implement its \char`\"{}physical
   representation\char`\"{} as (...) vectors of Constraints, as opposed to (...) vectors of pointers, it has to be {\itshape very} careful to {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} M\+O\+D\+I\+FY T\+H\+E\+SE V\+E\+C\+T\+O\+RS (in particular, increase their size) during the lifetime of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} in order to avoid the risk that some Constraints may change their memory location, hence their \char`\"{}name\char`\"{}.

A F\+O\+R\+T\+I\+O\+RI, S\+I\+ZE OF T\+HE \mbox{[}...\mbox{]} A\+R\+R\+A\+YS W\+H\+O\+SE P\+O\+I\+N\+T\+E\+RS A\+RE P\+R\+O\+V\+I\+D\+ED BY T\+H\+IS M\+E\+T\+H\+OD M\+U\+ST {\itshape N\+E\+V\+ER} BE C\+H\+A\+N\+G\+ED BY W\+H\+O\+M\+E\+V\+ER R\+E\+A\+DS T\+H\+EM. Since it must, conversely, be possible to change the individual Constraints, the arrays cannot be const (a size-\/cons, contents-\/mutable array should be used, which is possible but just too complicated at this point). It would be possible to enforce this for \mbox{[}...\mbox{]} arrays of {\itshape pointers}, but since it is (currently) not done for the others it\textquotesingle{}s not worth the bother; besides, resizing a vector of pointers do not change the address of the original objects (although hell may break loose for many other reasons).

Similarly, the size of the vector of static Constraints is {\itshape not} supposed to change along the life of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}\+: which {\itshape groups} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} are there is \char`\"{}the structure of the Block\char`\"{}, and this is assumed to be given. Individual Constraints can indeed appear and disappear, which is what dynamic Constraints are for, but \char`\"{}the set of indices of Constraints\char`\"{} is assumed to be given once and for all. That is, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a05ed7d7c9380f9476d53192bd2e4361d}{add\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraint()}} \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]} should only be called (by derived classes) during the initialization of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, and never thereafter. More precisely, because some \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} may not need to access the Constraints at all, the idea is that Constraints (be them static or dynamic) are only generated if and when they are actually required. This is what the methods \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}} and generate\+\_\+abstract\+\_\+constraints() are about. In particular, this method can only be called if generate\+\_\+abstract\+\_\+constraints() has been called, and the latter call may trigger a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_add9b4c918157bda66b5183f67953d49b}{generate\+\_\+static\+\_\+constraints()}}. However, once the sets of static and dynamic Constraints have been constructed, they are not supposed to change (except for addition/deletion of dynamic Constraints and changes in the Constraints that are handled by the appropriate Modifications). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6ca8b345bb506a8ac45eef39887bebea}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6ca8b345bb506a8ac45eef39887bebea}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!get\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables@{get\+\_\+static\+\_\+variables}}

\item a pointer to a boost\+::multi\+\_\+array$<$\+V $\ast$ , K$>$, i.\+e., of pointers of any class V that is either \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} or derived from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, in principle with any K (but the limit for K may be dictated by \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_ga8ec1a694aa7a5fa58ab801c1ea419fa8}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+static()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{group__boost__any__stuff_gab299af634bc1ca9be3a8d45a14560036}{un\+\_\+any\+\_\+thing()}} in \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}});
\end{DoxyItemize}

\index{is\+\_\+feasible@{is\+\_\+feasible}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{is\+\_\+feasible()}{is\_feasible()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual bool is\+\_\+feasible (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{bool}]{useabstract = {\ttfamily false},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{fsbc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



returns true if the current solution is (approximately) feasible 

Returns true if the solution encoded in the current value of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is approximately feasible within the given tolerances. This should always be reasonably cheap, even if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} encodes for an N\+P-\/hard problem.

The useabstract parameter dictates how the check should be performed\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if true, it should use the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (basically, check feasibility of all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}});
\item if false, it should rather use the data of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and the \char`\"{}logical\char`\"{} conditions that define feasibility in abstract, without using the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}.
\end{DoxyItemize}

The second version is useful for several reasons\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item it can be more computationally efficient;
\item it does not require the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to be even constructed;
\item it can serve as \char`\"{}debug\char`\"{} of the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} itself, in the sense that it gives a way to check that the feasible region as represented by the Constraints, that is checked by is\+\_\+feasible( true ), is the same as the feasible region that \char`\"{}logically\char`\"{} the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has, which is checked by is\+\_\+feasible( false ).
\end{DoxyItemize}

However, a significant difference exists between the two versions in case the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has dynamic constraints. Indeed, in that case the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} only contains those that have been explicitly generated so far, while the physical representation (logically) \char`\"{}contains
them all\char`\"{}. This means that is\+\_\+feasible( true ) may return true while is\+\_\+feasible( false ) may return false, as there might be dynamic constraints that have not been explicitly generated yet and that are violated by the current solution. This is not an issue, as it is always possible to call \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}} before \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a4204ffb542117f4ea97eeceeda4da079}{is\+\_\+feasible()}} to ensure that violated dynamic constraints, if any, are generated. Note that a call to is\+\_\+feasible( false ) may well rely on the same separation procedures as \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a306892932ce912f30548f3f3e4072e9b}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+constraints()}} to verify \char`\"{}logical\char`\"{} feasibility of the dynamic constraints; if the corresponding dynamic constraints are actually generated (a Block-\/specific decision), then is\+\_\+feasible( false ) and is\+\_\+feasible( true ), called in this order, should indeed give the same result.

The parameter fsbc, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object, is meant to specify \char`\"{}how much approximately feasible
the solution can be\char`\"{}. It can be a very simple quantity, such as a Simple\+Configuration$<$double$>$ specifying, say, the maximum relative violation that a simple single numerical \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} can have, or any arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} specifying different thresholds for different groups of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (say, via \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_simple_configuration}{Simple\+Configuration}}$<$std\+::vector$<$double$>$ $>$), and arbitrarily complex sub-\/\+Configurations (recursively) for the sub-\/\+Block of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

Note that the fsbc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with fsbc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the Block\+Config() is used. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}all Constraint must be satisfied
exactly\char`\"{}, which may be possible in some cases (say, a numerical \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} only producing \char`\"{}small\char`\"{} integer numbers).

The method is given a default implementation working for those Blocks for which feasibility is never an issue, in the sense that they are feasible if an only if all of its sub-\/\+Block are. This should basically mean that the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has no \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} on its own, and all \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} are in the sub-\/\+Block (which either mean that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is a separable problem, or that there are either linking \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} or linking nonlinear terms in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}). However, note that the default implementation only works for the sub-\/\+Block being called with the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}. For any other case the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to implement its own version; this cannot be done in the default implementation because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} fsbc for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will likely have to be \char`\"{}unpacked\char`\"{}, with each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} getting its own specific sub-\/\+Configuration, but this can only be done by a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} for a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, as the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class does not have direct support for the fact that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} contains a sub-\/\+Configuration for each sub-\/\+Block of a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a162cd7d8375d705f0b15d8e847098763}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!is\+\_\+optimal@{is\+\_\+optimal}}
\index{is\+\_\+optimal@{is\+\_\+optimal}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{is\+\_\+optimal()}{is\_optimal()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual bool is\+\_\+optimal (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{bool}]{useabstract = {\ttfamily false},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{optc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



$<$ returns true if the current solution is (approximately) optimal 

$<$ Returns true if the solution encoded in the current value of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be proven to be approximately optimal within the given tolerances. This might very well be a hard task, typically if the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} encodes for an N\+P-\/hard problem.

A case in which this is possible is if the problem is convex, since then a dual feasible solution satisfying the Complementary Slackness conditions with the \char`\"{}primal one\char`\"{} encoded in the current value of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} is a convenient \char`\"{}compact\char`\"{} optimality certificate. For such a case, this method can be taken as being equivalent to \char`\"{}is\+\_\+dual\+\_\+feasible()\char`\"{} (which means that the primal solution in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} may not actually be optimal, if it is either not feasible or does not satisfy the Complementary Slackness conditions).

The parameter dictates how the check should be performed\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item if true, it should use the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (like, checking dual feasibility using the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}});
\item if false, it should rather use the data of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and the \char`\"{}logical\char`\"{} conditions that define optimality in abstract (assuming there is any such thing), without using the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}.
\end{DoxyItemize}

The second version is useful for several reasons\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item it can be more computationally efficient;
\item it does not require the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to be even constructed;
\item it can serve as \char`\"{}debug\char`\"{} of the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} itself, in the sense that it gives a way to check that the optimality conditions using the \char`\"{}abstract\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, that are checked by is\+\_\+optimal( true ), coincide with those \char`\"{}logically\char`\"{} the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has, which are checked by is\+\_\+feasible( optimal ).
\end{DoxyItemize}

However, a significant difference exists between the two versions in case the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has dynamic variables. Indeed, in that case the abstract representation of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} only contains those that have been explicitly generated so far, while the physical representation (logically) \char`\"{}contains
them all\char`\"{}. This means that is\+\_\+optimal( true ) may return true while is\+\_\+optimal( false ) may return false, as there might be dynamic variables that have not been explicitly generated yet and that can be used to improve the value of the current solution. This is not an issue, as it is always possible to call \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} before \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a44ef42401ecae613574f3df619480de1}{is\+\_\+optimal()}} to ensure that useful dynamic variables, if any, are priced in. Note that a call to is\+\_\+optimal( false ) may well rely on the same pricing algorithm as \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ab90d0f60518eee75370e29c95bae4522}{generate\+\_\+dynamic\+\_\+variables()}} to verify \char`\"{}logical\char`\"{} optimality; if the corresponding dynamic variables are actually generated (a Block-\/specific decision), then is\+\_\+optimal( false ) and is\+\_\+optimal( true ), called in this order, should indeed give the same result.

The parameter optc, which is a pointer to an arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object, is meant to specify \char`\"{}how much approximately optimal
the solution can be\char`\"{}. It can be a very simple quantity, such as a Simple\+Configuration$<$double$>$ specifying, say, the maximum relative violation that any simple single numerical constraint in the {\itshape dual} of a convex problem may have, or, say, two such constants, one for dual constraint violation and another for Complementary Slackness violations. However, it can also be any arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}}, say containing arbitrarily complex sub-\/\+Configurations (recursively) for the sub-\/\+Block of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

Note that the optc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which means that if the method is called with optc = nullptr then the corresponding configuration from the Block\+Config() is used. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}the solution must be exactly
optimal\char`\"{}, which may be possible in some cases (say, a problem in which both the primal and the dual solutions only contain \char`\"{}small\char`\"{} integer numbers so that dual feasibility and Complementary Slackness can be verified without any numerical error).

The method is given a default implementation working for those Blocks for which optimality is never an issue, in the sense that they are optimal if an only if all of its sub-\/\+Block are. This is a \char`\"{}fake\char`\"{} implementation that basically only works if the problem is actually decomposable, hence one expects that true \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to implement their own version, not least because the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} optc for the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will likely have to be \char`\"{}unpacked\char`\"{}, with each inner \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} getting its own specific sub-\/\+Configuration, which cannot be done with the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} class. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a0b1e7ff87a97e692c97f52f096f6eb50}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{load()}{load()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void load (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{std\+::istream \&}]{input }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [protected]}, {\ttfamily [pure virtual]}}



load the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} out of an istream 

{\itshape pure virtual} method intended to provide support for Blocks to load themselves out of an istream. This is precisely what makes \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} an {\itshape abstract} base class\+: the actual content of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} depends on the specific derived class, which is why this method cannot be implemented. 

Implemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a221601b23896f7a98c6914f42224d446}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_simple_m_i_l_p_block_a019850055afe817b816bae5699f461d3}{Simple\+M\+I\+L\+P\+Block}}, and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_structured_m_i_l_p_block_a019850055afe817b816bae5699f461d3}{Structured\+M\+I\+L\+P\+Block}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+\+Modification()}{map\_back\_Modification()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void map\+\_\+back\+\_\+\+Modification (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{R3B,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga9024cf2ead61a1280d29771b063b1a62}{sp\+\_\+\+Mod}}}]{mod,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{r3bc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}}}]{issue\+P\+Mod = {\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4ada69e89c5f46e563d8a13cfa6830c136}{e\+No\+Blck}}},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}}}]{issue\+A\+Mod = {\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4a21b14b26b1c0093a0905f6fc010bad29}{e\+Mod\+Blck}}} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



maps back a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} from an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Once a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has been produced \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} above\mbox{]}, it is a completely independent object from the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that created it. Hence, any modification to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does not affect the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Also, because the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may be \char`\"{}very different\char`\"{} from the original one, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} in the former may be nontrivial (or even impossible) to map into one or more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to the latter that keep the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \char`\"{}in sync\char`\"{} with its R3 one.

Because this may nonetheless be desirable in many scenarios, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides this method to support the case in which specific Modificatios to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should be applied \char`\"{}verbatim\char`\"{} to its original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. This actually means that the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will be subject to some changes that \char`\"{}have the same effect of the original Modification\char`\"{}, whatever this exactly means for this original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and assuming this is possible at all), so that specific \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} will be issued to any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

R3B is assumed to be a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} produced by the current one of \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} r3bc (the same, or identical, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object used in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} to produce R3B in the first place). mod is assumed to be a (smart) pointer to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object that applies to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

Mapping a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} from another \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} likely involves some new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to be issued by the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and/or its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_function}{Function}}, ...). The two parameters issue\+P\+Mod and issue\+A\+Mod control how this is done for \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{} respectively, with the format of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a2d499ae38e5e95cf66cd71da32b0694a}{Observer\+::make\+\_\+par()}}. Note that for \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} the setting \char`\"{}e\+Mod\+Block\char`\"{} makes no sense because a \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} is never a concern of the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that has issued it; in fact, its default value is e\+No\+Blck, as opposed to e\+Mod\+Blck for that of the \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{}. Note that the \char`\"{}channel names\char`\"{} in the parameters obviously have to refer to channels {\itshape of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}} (as opposed to as of R3B), because it is here that the new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} (if any) will be issued.

As mapping a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} may be a rather complex (if at all possible) task, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may not support all possible mappings. If the required operation is not supported, and exception will be thrown. The default implementation of the method works for \char`\"{}extremely lazy\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} not being willing to implement any of the possible mapping, or extremely unlucky ones not having any workable one to implement. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a80212ada24a69dc8a0590eb82b5ef7c4}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\index{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution@{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}{map\_back\_solution()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{R3B,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{r3bc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{solc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



maps back solution information from an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Once a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has been produced \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} above\mbox{]}, it will be typically necessary to map solution information back from the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to the original one. This method is assumed to be exactly this\+: R3B is assumed to be a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} produced by the current one of \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} r3bc (the same, or identical, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object used in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} to produce R3B in the first place), and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should map back the solution information contained into the Variables of R3B into its own.

Note that in general the mapping can be highly nontrivial\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} in the R3B can be less than the ones in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (in case of a Restriction), but can even be a completely different set (in case of a Reformulation that expresses the feasible region in an entirely different way, think e.\+g. of the Dantzig-\/\+Wolfe reformulation). Hence the mapping may not even be easy to write algebraically; yet, this is not a problem since it is implemented algorithmically in this method.

This method supports the general notion that \char`\"{}not all the solution might
be required\char`\"{}, i.\+e., that a partial map (say, only some of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}) may only be required. This is why the method has the third parameter solc, which is intended to allow the caller to restrict the map to only some subset of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}. Because, as usual, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can itself have multiple \char`\"{}sets\char`\"{} of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, and also have sub-\/\+Block (recursively) each of which can in turn have many ones, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object is required to be able to specify any arbitrarily complex subset of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}.

Actually, it may make sense in several scenarios that the thusly mapped back solution information is then saved with a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a1db67c2f65111af4314e79dfc81ba597}{get\+\_\+\+Solution()}}. This is why the solc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which is shared between this method, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a1db67c2f65111af4314e79dfc81ba597}{get\+\_\+\+Solution()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}}. That is, if the method is called with solc = nullptr then the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} from the Block\+Config() is used. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}map them back all\char`\"{}. If the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and hence, hopefully, the R3B) also has dual information attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} together than solution information stored in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, this should be taken to mean \char`\"{}copy both solution and dual
information\char`\"{}.

A specific twist of mapping back solutions is that the R3B may have, after having been constructed, independently generated dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and/or \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} above\mbox{]}. If it has generated dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} that are not present in the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, then it may not be possible for the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to fully map back the solution in the R3B (although it may also be possible, depending on exactly how the map is done). In this case, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} is assumed to do a best effort attempt to copy as much as possible of the solution, but there is no guarantee that the copied solution will be equivalent (in particular, it may not be feasible even if the one in the R3B would have been so). A similar case happens with dual variables of dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, assuming they exist. However, it should always be possible to ensure that a full map exists by ensuring that the dynamic Variable/\+Constraint of the R3B and of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are \char`\"{}in sync\char`\"{} {\itshape before} calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}}. This should always be possible by using map\+\_\+\mbox{[}back/forward\mbox{]}\+\_\+modification() \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]}, of course assuming that the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} supports the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}}. Alternatively, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may in principle be capable of doing the adding of Variable/\+Constraint on-\/the-\/fly during the call to this method, i.\+e., generate in itself all the Variable/\+Constraint that are present in the R3B and that are necessary for the mapping to work; of course, in this case the appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} should be issued.

Note that the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to be able to access the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and to its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} if dual information also has to be mapped). This is obvious in case of a copy (as the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be expected to be of the exact same class as the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}), but not so otherwise. Hence, the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will either have to make the (specific derived class of) the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} friend, or will have to provide methods for accessing all the necessary Variable/\+Constraint, or to make them public outright.

The method is given an extremely lazy default implementation refusing to map back solution from any kind of R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, comprised the \char`\"{}copy\char`\"{} one. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_aaab5f3727d85b0084832d6956470f21f}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a26eeed482e287e1f8a384826c789f9e0}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a26eeed482e287e1f8a384826c789f9e0}} 
\index{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}!map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification@{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification}}
\index{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification@{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification()}{map\_forward\_Modification()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+\+Modification (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{R3B,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga9024cf2ead61a1280d29771b063b1a62}{sp\+\_\+\+Mod}}}]{mod,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{r3bc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}}}]{issue\+P\+Mod = {\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4ada69e89c5f46e563d8a13cfa6830c136}{e\+No\+Blck}}},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_ga544246d8c462e9a2d817c2c30797968f}{c\+\_\+\+Mod\+Param}}}]{issue\+A\+Mod = {\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{group___modification___t_y_p_e_s_gga48fddcdd03a03f49b7e389ca1ed938d4a21b14b26b1c0093a0905f6fc010bad29}{e\+Mod\+Blck}}} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



maps forward a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} from the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to an R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Once a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has been produced \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} above\mbox{]}, it is a completely independent object from the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that created it. Hence, any modification to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does not affect the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Also, because the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may be \char`\"{}very different\char`\"{} from the original one, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} in the latter may be nontrivial (or even impossible) to map into one or more \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to the former that keep the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \char`\"{}in sync\char`\"{} with its original one.

Because this may nonetheless be desirable in many scenarios, the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides this method to support the case in which specific modifications to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should be applied \char`\"{}verbatim\char`\"{} to some R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of its. This actually means that the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will be subject to some changes that \char`\"{}have the same effect of the original Modification\char`\"{}, whatever this exactly means for this R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and assuming this is possible at all), so that specific \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} will be issued to any \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

R3B is assumed to be a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} produced by the current one of \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} r3bc (the same, or identical, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object used in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} to produce R3B in the first place). mod is assumed to be a (smart) pointer to a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} object that applies to the current \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}.

One example of use of such a mechanism is when the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has dynamic Variable/\+Constraint, but the R3B lacks the corresponding pricing/separation methods and cannot therefore generate them itself. It is, however, possible to \char`\"{}simulate\char`\"{} the generation of Variable/\+Constraint by the R3B as follows\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item copy the solution (primal and/or dual as it need be) from the R3B into the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} above\mbox{]};
\item have the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} generate whatever new dynamic Variable/\+Constraint as dictated by the imported solution;
\item intercept the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} and use this method to have the same new Variable/\+Constraint be added to the R3B.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that \char`\"{}intercepting\char`\"{} the Modifications is only possible for a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} attached to the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. However, any number of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} can be attached to a given \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, even if they are not actually used to solve it (but only exploit being attached to snoop on the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} occurring in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}).

Mapping a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to another \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} likely involves some new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} to be issued by that \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and/or its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_objective}{Objective}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_function}{Function}}, ...). The two parameters issue\+P\+Mod and issue\+A\+Mod control how this is done for \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} and \char`\"{}abstract
\+Modification\char`\"{} respectively, with the format of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a2d499ae38e5e95cf66cd71da32b0694a}{Observer\+::make\+\_\+par()}}. Note that for \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} the setting \char`\"{}e\+Mod\+Block\char`\"{} makes no sense because a \char`\"{}physical Modification\char`\"{} is never a concern of the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} that has issued it; in fact, its default value is e\+No\+Blck, as opposed to e\+Mod\+Blck for that of the \char`\"{}abstract Modification\char`\"{}. Note that the \char`\"{}channel
names\char`\"{} in the parameters obviously have to refer to channels {\itshape of the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} R3B}, because it is there that the new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} (if any) will be issued.

As mapping a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} may be a rather complex (if at all possible) task, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may not support all possible mappings. If the required operation is not supported, and exception will be thrown. The default implementation of the method works for \char`\"{}extremely lazy\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} not being willing to implement any of the possible mapping, or extremely unlucky ones not having any workable one to implement. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a97c60b41fa6562804000b7324576ae31}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}}

\index{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution@{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution}!S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block@{S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+di\+\_\+unipi\+\_\+it\+::\+Block}}
\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}{map\_forward\_solution()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} $\ast$}]{R3B,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{r3bc = {\ttfamily nullptr},  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} $\ast$}]{solc = {\ttfamily nullptr} }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



maps forward solution information from the original Blocka to n R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Once a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has been produced \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} above\mbox{]}, it might be useful to map solution information from the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, the reverse of what \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}} \mbox{[}see above\mbox{]} does. This method is assumed to be exactly this\+: R3B is assumed to be a R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} produced by the current one of \char`\"{}type\char`\"{} r3bc (the same, or identical, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object used in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a2e682f5c0d4a74503c0cb5ab38fb6428}{get\+\_\+\+R3\+\_\+\+Block()}} to produce R3B in the first place), and the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} should map the solution information contained into its own \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} into these of the R3B. Similar observations apply as in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}}\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item In general the mapping can be highly nontrivial to write algebraically, bit this is not a problem since it is implemented algorithmically.
\item This method supports the general notion that \char`\"{}not all the solution might
  be required\char`\"{}, i.\+e., that a partial map (say, only some of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}) may only be required. This is why the method has the third parameter solc, and arbitrarily complex \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} object which is intended to allow the caller to restrict the map to only some subset of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} (among the possibly many different ones of the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} and those of each of its sub-\/\+Block, recursively). Again, the solc parameter is meant as an {\itshape override} of the default \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} for this task set by means of \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a18d411b740a47db00d15083c5c7ef2ba}{set\+\_\+\+Block\+Config()}}, which is shared between this method, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a1db67c2f65111af4314e79dfc81ba597}{get\+\_\+\+Solution()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a04f0a8a608fcc3ded227c2552f67f1a3}{map\+\_\+back\+\_\+solution()}}. That is, if the method is called with solc = nullptr then the corresponding \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_configuration}{Configuration}} from the Block\+Config() is used. If the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_config}{Block\+Config}} is not set (nullptr) or the corresponding field is not set (nullptr), this is assumed to mean \char`\"{}map them forward all\char`\"{}. If the R3B (and hence, hopefully, the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}) also has dual information attached to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} together than solution information stored in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}}, this should be taken to mean \char`\"{}copy both solution and dual information\char`\"{}.
\item After having created the R3B, the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may have generated dynamic \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} and/or \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} that are not present in the R3B. This may (or may not, depending on exactly how the map is done) make it impossible to map forward the full solution. In this case, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}} is assumed to do a best effort attempt to copy as much as possible of the solution, but there is no guarantee that the copied solution will be equivalent; a similar case happens with dual information. However, it should always be possible to ensure that a full map exists by ensuring that the dynamic Variable/\+Constraint of the R3B and of the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} are \char`\"{}in sync\char`\"{} {\itshape before} calling \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a7759467d70613a10c1cdb5d3193b4434}{map\+\_\+forward\+\_\+solution()}}, which should always be possible by using map\+\_\+\mbox{[}back/forward\mbox{]}\+\_\+modification() \mbox{[}see below\mbox{]}. Alternatively, the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} may in principle be capable of adding to the R3B the Variable/\+Constraint that are necessary for the mapping to work on-\/the-\/fly during the call to this method, in which case the appropriate \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_modification}{Modification}} should be issued.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note that the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will have to be able to access the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} of the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and to its \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} if dual information also has to be mapped). This is obvious in case of a copy (as the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} can be expected to be of the exact same class as the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}), but not so otherwise. Hence, the R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will either have to make the (specific derived class of) the original \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} friend, or will have to provide methods for accessing all the necessary Variable/\+Constraint, or to make them public outright.

The method is given an extremely lazy default implementation refusing to map forward solution from any kind of R3 \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, comprised the \char`\"{}copy\char`\"{} one. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_af990663a09f7203179f903e91c6bb3f1}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}.

getting the classname of this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} 

Given a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, this method returns a string with its class name; unlike std\+::type\+\_\+info.\+name(), there {\itshape are} guarantees, i.\+e., the name will always be the same.

The method works by dispatching the private virtual method private\+\_\+name(). The latter is automatically implemented by the S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+insert\+\_\+in\+\_\+factory\+\_\+cpp\+\_\+$\ast$ macros \mbox{[}see \mbox{\hyperlink{_s_m_s_typedefs_8h}{S\+M\+S\+Typedefs.\+h}}\mbox{]}, hence this comes at no cost since these have to be called somewhere to ensure that any \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} will be added to the factory. Actually, since Block\+::private\+\_\+name() is pure virtual, this ensures that it is not possible to forget to call the appropriate S\+M\+Spp\+\_\+insert\+\_\+in\+\_\+factory\+\_\+cpp\+\_\+$\ast$ for any \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} because otherwise it is a pure virtual class (unless the programmer purposely defines private\+\_\+name() without calling the macro, which seems rather pointless). \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae8c20577eae73143d71b37b1550c1e93}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_ae8c20577eae73143d71b37b1550c1e93}}

create a new level in the given channel 

This method allows to nest a new \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} into the existing \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} associated with the given channel.

When it is invoked, a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} object is (created, unless it is provided, and it is) appended at the end of the S\+TL container in the \char`\"{}outer\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} associated to the channel. Then, a call to add\+\_\+\+Modification( mod , chnl ) adds mod to the \char`\"{}inner\char`\"{} \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}. This lasts until the method is called again, on which case an inner-\/inner-\/\+Group\+Modification is started (...), or either un\+\_\+nest\+\_\+channel( chnl ) or close\+\_\+channel( chnl ) are called.

The parameters gmpmod and issue\+Mod have the same meaning as in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a6459343d63aec3d39ece1e20467db47b}{open\+\_\+channel()}} and are provided for the same reason.

Calling the method with chnl non being the name of an open channel is an error and should throw exception. 

Implements \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_a4869c34f8f2a2286c39fdce0521b93c9}{Observer}}.

construct a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} of specific type using the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} factory 

Use the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} factory to construct a \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} object of type specified by classname (a std\+::string with the name of the class inside). If there is no class with the given name, exception is thrown. The optional parameter father is the pointer to the father \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}, that (if provided) is passed to the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} constructor inside the factory.

Note that the method is static because the factory is static, hence it is to be called as \begin{DoxyVerb}  Block *myBlock = Block::new_Block( someclass );

i.\+e., without any reference to any specific \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} (and, therefore, it can be used to construct the very first \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} if needed).

Note that the \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} returned my this method is \char`\"{}empty\char`\"{}\+: it contains no (instance) data, and therefore it has to be explicitly initialized with any of the corresponding methods (operator$>$$>$, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a8209df4072912666c1ea5f6979c035fc}{serialize()}}, anything that the specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class provides) before it can be used.

to its definition (typically, in the private part in its .h file);

\end{DoxyVerb}


to exactly {\itshape one} .cpp file, typically that \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} .cpp file. 
\end{DoxyItemize}\mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a47c09b44e75478c5f3e24323d2f6449f}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a47c09b44e75478c5f3e24323d2f6449f}}

\char`\"{}open\char`\"{} a channel 

This method allows to start \char`\"{}bunching together\char`\"{} a set of \char`\"{}logically
related Modification\char`\"{}.

When it is invoked, a \char`\"{}new channel is opened\char`\"{}. This means that a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} object is (created, unless it is provided, and it is) assigned a new unique name, which is returned. Then, being chnl the returned value, a call to add\+\_\+\+Modification( mod , chnl ) adds mod at the end of the S\+TL container of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}, rather than dispatching it to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} / \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. The latter operaton is done when close\+\_\+channel( chnl ) is called.

If the parameter gmpmod is != nullptr, then the pointed object is taken as the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} that is \char`\"{}opened\char`\"{}. This means that the object becomes \char`\"{}property\char`\"{} of the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer}{Observer}}; the raw pointer is later packaged in a smart pointer (when the channel is closed or flushed), so it is crucial that no copies of the raw pointer are retained. This is done in order to allow the caller to provide objects of {\itshape derived classes} from \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}; these may contain other data that is useful to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} / \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to process the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}, and even just being of a specific \+:\mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} class may help. If gmpmod is == nullptr, an object of the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} class is automatically constructed by the method.

If gmpmod is == nullptr, the parameter issue\+Mod decides what is the concerns\+\_\+\+Block() value of the newly created \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}}\+:


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item e\+No\+Blck the concerns\+\_\+\+Block() value is set to false, meaning that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} receiving this \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_group_modification}{Group\+Modification}} (when it is eventually finalized with close\+\_\+\+Group\+Modification() ) can safely ignore it on knowledge that the corresponding change in the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} has already happened;
\item e\+Mod\+Blck the concerns\+\_\+\+Block() value is set to true; this is the default, \char`\"{}most conservative\char`\"{} setting. 
\end{DoxyItemize}

Implements \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_observer_acf7e46d450779f524e3655c5f31eecc8}{Observer}}.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{print()}{print()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily virtual void print (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{std\+::ostream \&}]{output }\end{DoxyParamCaption}) const\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [protected]}, {\ttfamily [virtual]}}



print information about the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} on an ostream with the given verbosity 

Protected method intended to print information about the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}; it is virtual so that derived classes can print their specific information in the format they choose. The level of the verbosity of the printed information is defined by the verbosity\+\_\+lvl field of the class; in particular, the \char`\"{}complete\char`\"{} level is is assumed to save enough information to allow a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} to completely re-\/read its structure from the file. This makes little sense for the base \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} class, in that it has no real structure of its own to save. 

Reimplemented in \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_m_c_f_block_a617885c1d228e0ca67bbb0efdbe713e3}{M\+C\+F\+Block}}, \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_simple_m_i_l_p_block_a59a9fcbf8f9f86d348344b2073644b0e}{Simple\+M\+I\+L\+P\+Block}}, and \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_structured_m_i_l_p_block_a59a9fcbf8f9f86d348344b2073644b0e}{Structured\+M\+I\+L\+P\+Block}}.

Method for adding a \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} to the set of those currently registered with the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}}. Note that the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block}{Block}} does {\itshape not} set itself to the \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_solver}{Solver}} (we assume the converse is done, see \mbox{\hyperlink{_solver_8h}{Solver.\+h}}). Note that the method is virtual because derived classes may have to do more. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a10f535001a82e7e9950f3a69967ae56e}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a10f535001a82e7e9950f3a69967ae56e}}

This method removes the given \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_constraint}{Constraint}} from each \mbox{\hyperlink{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_variable}{Variable}} that is active in it. \mbox{\Hypertarget{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a70a454edfce317205e2b0e65823a8322}\label{class_s_m_spp__di__unipi__it_1_1_block_a70a454edfce317205e2b0e65823a8322}}
